# Task checklist

Rules: see [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md). Claim a task by filling in `owner` (name + branch) before
starting. Status: `[ ]` open · `[~]` in progress / partly done · `[x]` done · `[-]` dropped.
Priority: **P1** = needed for a usable public beta, P2 = important, P3 = nice to have.
A new feature task names the use case it serves (`serves: 1, 3`; the numbered list in [PRODUCT.md](PRODUCT.md#use-cases-to-design-for)).

- [x] **T-002 P1** Same check for Bambu Studio and PrusaSlicer (Prusa uses `Metadata/Slic3r_PE_model.config`). · owner: Claude (`task/T-001-slicer-check`) — Bambu Studio passes as is; PrusaSlicer passes after adding `Slic3r_PE_model.config` with its key names (`PRUSA_KEYS`).
- [~] **T-003 P1** Real-part corpus: collect ~20 typical hobby STLs/STEPs with known good orientations (licence permitting), add to `core/tests/corpus/`, write a regression test for top orientation + key findings. · owner: Claude (`task/T-020-knowledge`) — 10 generated stand-ins (`tools/make_corpus.py`: shelf bracket, knob, pencil cup, drawer handle, sign, pegboard hook, spool hub, tray, tall pin, gear) with expected top pick and must / must-not findings (`core/tests/test_corpus.py`). Left: real downloaded parts (Printables blocks automated downloads; needs licence-checked CC0/CC-BY files picked by hand).
- [x] **T-190 P2** Orientation search: offer "lying down" for round pins and rods. · owner: Claude (`task/T-190-lying-pins`) — lying down is scored but rightly loses as modelled: a round pin touches the bed in a line and needs support along its length (score 5+ vs 1.7). Added a stability term (tall on a narrow base, same limit as the tipping check) so a standing option says why it's risky. A "flat along one side" design change would make lying down win; no solid source found for it yet (only maker listings), so not suggested.
- [x] **T-191 P1** Keep the computer usable while simulations run. · owner: Claude (`task/T-190-lying-pins`) — the browser engine is one web worker (one core); `mcp_server.py` caps OMP/OpenBLAS/MKL threads at half the cores (user settings win); rule recorded in PRODUCT.md.
- [~] **T-004 P1** Browser performance on big parts (200k–1M triangles): measure load/orient/check/strength times, fix the worst. · owner: Claude (`task/T-004-performance`) — 572k-face CAD export in Pyodide: load 12→1.6 s, check ~50→16 s, orient 8 s (`node tools/pyodide_check.mjs part.stl`). Fixes: outlines simplified to 0.01 mm, vectorised ring building, no vertex dedupe in placement, `to_bytes()` for uploads. Same part, native: face list 50→4 s, design 59→16 s (Pyodide 99→33 s), strength 29→3 s, stronger options 84→6 s; strength no longer fails on voxel islands. 2026-10-31 orient on a 1.08M-face part natively 12.7 → 3.8 s (same pick): cached mesh bounds/volume (were recomputed 31×), integer keys to group face normals, finalists re-scored on a 200k-face area sample. In Pyodide (`node tools/pyodide_check.mjs`, 1.08M faces): load 2.5 s, orient 5.1 s (was ~12 s), check 11.4 s, design 45.5 s. Left: the design suggestions (45 s) and the check (11 s) on huge parts.

Left: PLA+ layer bond and TPU stiffness are estimates (marked verify). 2026-11-20 leftover done: PLA+ layer factor 0.6 now sourced (eSUN PLA+: 31.2 MPa across the layers vs 53.34 along them = 0.58); TPU 95A stiffness 60 → 30 MPa (Polymaker PolyFlex TPU95: Young's modulus 29 ± 2.8 MPa, ISO 527; 33.6 MPa in the V5.5 sheet).

Left: real prints to update the built-in factors.
- [x] **T-120 P1** Simple level: walls, top/bottom layers and infill as inputs in the strength panel (used for the 3MF too); top/bottom shells modelled by build direction, walls on the sides. · files: `strength.py`, `api.py`, `web/app.js` · owner: Claude (`task/T-120-simple-sim`) — `Shells`; depth to the surface sideways (walls × line width), up (top layers × layer height) and down (bottom); "How it's printed" fields; defaults from the goal. Orientation ranking still uses walls all round (one shared solve).
- [x] **T-121 P1** Optimise settings for the user's load: search walls, top/bottom, infill and orientation for the target safety, cheapest by the chosen goal (balanced / strength / speed / material). · owner: Claude (`task/T-120-simple-sim`) — `optimise_strength`: from a light print, add the step with the most safety per gram until ×2 (×3 for the strength goal); this orientation and the strongest; stops at once when even solid isn't enough. Cost is grams for every goal (time follows plastic closely; per-goal time cost left for later). ~25 s native on the hook.
- [x] **T-122 P1** Plain-language strength results: where it breaks, why, what the suggestion changes, in one sentence each. · owner: Claude (`task/T-120-simple-sim`) — `explanation` on strength and optimiser results (verdict, height of the weak spot, layers vs plastic and what helps).
- [~] **T-123 P1** Simple heat check: heat build-up per height from layer times (small pins / tips that stay soft) and a warping estimate per region; heat map in the viewer. · owner: Claude (`task/T-120-simple-sim`) — `heat.py`: 1-D column of layers (nozzle heat on small layers, fan, bed, slicer slow-down floored at 10 mm/s), temperature 1 mm below the top vs the material's softening point (Orca profiles) → `small_layers_cooling` finding with °C, "Show heat while printing" colours. Margins set on test shapes (Ø3 mm pin flagged, 10 mm tower and block not); needs real prints. Left: warping stays the footprint-length check; a warp map comes with the G-code level (T-126).
- [x] **T-124 P2** Detailed level (local): slice with OrcaSlicer, read the G-code toolpaths into voxels (wall / infill / top-bottom, line direction, time printed). · owner: Claude (`task/T-124-gcode-sim`) — `gcode.py` (numpy only, so it runs in the browser too): Orca/Bambu/Prusa/Cura feature comments, absolute/relative E, arcs, layer detection from the part's own lines, times scaled to the slicer's estimate; matched to the model by size and a quarter turn on the bed (wrong part refused). Web: load a G-code or `.gcode.3mf` in the strength panel; MCP `detailed_strength` slices with the installed OrcaSlicer. Hook: 0.4 s read + 4 s check in Pyodide.
- [x] **T-125 P2** Detailed strength from G-code: per-voxel direction-dependent material (along lines / across / between layers), finer grid. · owner: Claude (`task/T-124-gcode-sim`) — real plastic per cube (normalised by the share of the cube inside the part; infill smoothed over 3×3×3), main line direction and how aligned the lines are; new failure mode "lines" (lines pulled apart sideways, bond = layer bond until calibrated). Agrees with the simple level on a sliced test part (×2.11 vs ×2.15); solid plate pulled across its lines holds about half. Finer grid = the existing accuracy check. Left: calibrate the across-lines strength with prints.
- [x] **T-126 P2** Detailed heat and warping from G-code timing: heat added line by line, cooling, shrinkage → warp map, corner lift, overheating spots. · owner: Claude (`task/T-124-gcode-sim`) — heat check uses the real time between layers and the plastic in each from the G-code (`gcode.layer_times`). 2026-11-01 **line by line** (`gcode.spot_heat`): every 2 mm spot of every layer gets the time until the next layer covers it (median print time of the lines within 6 mm, so one line ending next to the next layer's start doesn't count) and a temperature then (exponential cooling, time constant set so a spot covered after the filament's minimum layer time sits at the heat check's warn margin); the detailed check colours each face by its spot ("Show heat while printing") and reports a hot spot the whole-layer model missed. **Warping with the G-code's timing**: layers that come quickly are still warm (layer heat model with the real layer times) and shrink less (`warp.simulate(warm=…)`, `api.warping(detailed=True)`, used when "use my G-code" is on); the warp map and lift spots come from that run.
- [x] **T-130 P1** Strength-aware orientation picks the weak direction: mushroom pulled on its stem → suggests printing on the cap (stem upright, layers pulled apart) instead of on its side. · owner: Claude (`task/T-130-sim-fixes`) — strength only counted below ×2; now a penalty per halving vs the strongest direction (by goal), the strongest is always offered, reasons say "holds 1.8× more". Strength goal: side wins.
- [x] **T-131 P1** FEA-style stress view: smooth colours over the part (blue → red) with a legend in MPa / % of strength, like normal FEA software. · owner: Claude (`task/T-130-sim-fixes`) — refined display mesh, banded rainbow, legend; % of strength / von Mises / displacement; exaggerated bending; section cut with the analysis cubes.
- [x] **T-132 P1** Load symbols are hidden by the part when behind it (lock, arrows). · owner: Claude (`task/T-130-sim-fixes`)
- [x] **T-133 P2** Set the force direction on the part: drag the arrow / angle controls, any direction, not only push/pull/down. · owner: Claude (`task/T-130-sim-fixes`) — trackball drag of the arrow; turn/tilt sliders (↻ on the load).
- [x] **T-134 P2** Review the strength simulation against standard FEA practice (mesh check, convergence, deformation view, reaction forces) and fix what is missing. · owner: Claude (`task/T-130-sim-fixes`) — singularities: 2 element rings at supports/loads left out, weak spot next to a clamp called out; "check accuracy" re-runs on a 3× finer grid and says how stable the answer is (samples: 14–25 % lower → shown honestly); grid size, total load and solver state shown; deformation view. Still open: self-weight, contact, non-linear material (out of scope for hobby checks).
- [x] **T-138 P1** Supports and loads only acted on a few voxels of big CAD faces (sampled at triangle corners/centres): hot spots on the held face, wrong weak spot. · owner: Claude (`task/T-130-sim-fixes`) — faces sampled every h/2; all nodes of the mushroom's top face held (was 48 of 1764).
- [x] **T-139 P2** "How is it held?": rigidly clamped/glued vs resting on a surface (only pushes back) vs a screw in a hole; a rigid glued face overstates stress at its edge. · owner: Claude (`task/T-139-hold`) — `hold` on fixed loads: resting and screw are stiff springs along the surface normal that only push (released where the part pulls away, re-solved; a little friction sideways), clamped as before. A ledge under a beam carries it pushed down and drops out pulled up; a part that only rests and is pulled off is refused. Picker preselects "screw" on holes; AI and MCP schemas.
- [x] **T-033 P3** Partial-voxel fill fractions for better accuracy on thin sections. · files: `voxel.py`, `strength.py` · owner: Claude (`task/T-033-partial-voxels`) — strength grids record each cube's share inside the part (exact along the rays, 1/3 cube across) and keep cubes ≥ 25 % inside; stiffness, strength, weight and the walls / top / bottom layers follow it, and opposite shells add up in one cube. 1 mm vs 2 mm strip: 1.45× (hand calc 1.43×, binary 1.0×); mushroom volume error 9 % → 0.1 %.

## Design edits

- [x] **T-135 P1** Missing suggestions (mushroom): round sharp corners of the bed footprint against warping, chamfer the bottom edge against elephant foot, fillet/chamfer where the strength check finds stress at an inside corner (stem base). · owner: Claude (`task/T-130-sim-fixes`) — rounded corners (also PLA when the footprint ≥ 30 % of its warp length), 0.5 mm 45° bottom chamfer, 45° fillet along the inside corner nearest the weak spot (mushroom: ×3.1 → ×3.6 with 2 mm, ×4.4 with 4 mm). Fillet pieces leave small gaps at the stem's outside corners.
- [~] **T-136 P2** Slicer helpers users don't know about: hole compensation / polyholes for holes, elephant foot compensation, … recommended with plain reasons when the part needs them. · owner: Claude (`task/T-130-sim-fixes`) — polyholes with hole compensation, elephant foot compensation by default. Verified with `tools/slicer_check.py` (plate with a Ø6 hole): OrcaSlicer applies polyholes per object (33 → 17 corners), Bambu Studio ignores the key (no polyholes there), both apply elephant-foot compensation; the reason shown says so. Left: more helpers (ironing, seam placement, precise walls) → T-151 / the wizard.

- [x] **T-040 P2** Chamfer under flat overhangs (replace a flat ceiling edge by 45°). · files: `features.py` · owner: Claude (`task/T-040-design-edits`) — `additions.py`: 45° fill under ledges up to `ledge_max_mm` wide (mitred at corners; under a too-long bridge it makes a pointed arch). - [x] **T-041 P2** Thicken thin walls to 2–3 line widths. · owner: Claude (`task/T-040-design-edits`) — walls under `wall_lines` lines grow on both sides, kept clear of nearby material so gaps don't fuse.
- [x] **T-042 P2** Sacrificial bridge layer for counterbores / holes starting mid-air. · owner: Claude (`task/T-040-design-edits`) — new `hole_starts_midair` finding + one-layer cover edit. Roofs held all round now count as bridges when narrow enough.
- [x] **T-043 P2** Adjustable edit parameters in the UI (chamfer size, teardrop angle). · owner: Claude (`task/T-010-refine`) — `design(params=…)`, inputs in the design panel, MCP `params`. Chamfers on short edges are capped so two don't meet in a new sharp point.
- [x] **T-044 P2** Feature confirm UI ("is this a hole?", tag ribs/bosses), feeding detection. · owner: Claude (`task/T-040-design-edits`) — round holes/posts listed with Yes / Not a hole (unsure ones marked ?); `tag_feature` (API, MCP, Ask AI). ### Agent toolkit (Phase 11, PLAN section 6)

The app has no AI built in. An agent the user already runs (Claude Code, Codex, Claude Desktop) drives the
tool through the MCP server or the command line and gets reports, charts and pictures back as files.

- [x] **T-260 P2** Remove the built-in AI: the Ask-AI panel, own-key settings and paste-back (`web/assistant.js`, `gemini.js`, `openai.js`, the `?ai=1` code, their e2e steps and docs). Keep "Copy for your AI chat" as a plain report. · owner: Claude (`task/T-260-remove-ai`) — Ask-AI panel, own-key settings and paste-back gone (`assistant.js`, `gemini.js`, `openai.js`, the `?ai=1` switch, their CSS and e2e step); "Copy for your AI chat" copies the plain Markdown report (no answer format), e2e checks it.
- [x] **T-261 P2** Every API call as an MCP tool: the MCP server covers `api.METHODS` (zones, box heat, nozzle comparison, Expert settings, warping, …) instead of a hand-picked list; one test that fails when a new API method has no tool. · owner: Claude (`task/T-261-mcp-all`) — 14 new tools (check_orientation, compare_nozzles, check_warping, load_gcode, heat_curve, box_heat, add_zone, place_box, apply_blocks, snap_model, strength_variant, set_custom_presets, calibration_part, expert / set_expert_settings); `zones` on check_strength / check_dynamics, `keep_clear` on the design tools; `mcp_server.API_TOOLS` maps every `api.METHODS` entry to its tool and `test_every_api_method_has_a_tool` fails when one is missing.
- [x] **T-262 P2** Pictures without a browser: the engine renders PNG images of the part (numpy software renderer, no new dependency): on the bed, problem close-ups, overhang map, stress colours with load arrows, warp shape, before / after a design change. MCP `render_image` returns the file path (and the image). · owner: Claude (`task/T-262-render`) — `core/fdmcore/render.py`: numpy software renderer (orthographic, flat shading, vectorised z-buffer, 2× supersampling, PNG via zlib; runs in Pyodide too). `render.picture(view, up, …)`: bed, overhangs (from below, coloured like the app), problem close-up (finding's area in red), stress (FEA colours, held faces blue, force arrows), warp (curl drawn at 5 % of the part's size), compare (earlier version beside the current one). ~1–8 s at 800 × 600. 2026-11-07 MCP `render_image` (saves the PNG and returns it as an image the agent can look at) and `fdmdfm render --view …`.
- [x] **T-263 P2** Charts as SVG from the engine: safety factor per orientation, print time / weight per nozzle and setting, warping per corner, settings sweeps. Same colours as the app. · owner: Claude (`task/T-263-charts`) — `core/fdmcore/charts.py` (plain SVG strings, runs in Pyodide too, app colours): safety per orientation coloured against ×2 (or support area without loads), print time and filament per nozzle, warping against the longest flat bar (the simulation gives no lift per corner, so not per corner), strength vs. temperature, safety vs. a setting (sweep). Agents get them through the report and its MCP / CLI tools (T-264).

- [x] **T-150 P2** (Phase 8) Custom infill: density per region from the stress result; strengthening holes that make the slicer add walls inside; 3MF modifier volumes (Orca/Bambu/Prusa) to strengthen or lighten regions. · owner: Claude (`task/T-150-zones`) — zones: boxes around the most stressed cubes (upright on the bed), more walls/infill there (`Shells.zones`), the optimiser adds zone steps and reports the plastic saved (cantilever bar: 7 g instead of 8 g); exported as modifier volumes — OrcaSlicer/Bambu need component objects, PrusaSlicer triangle ranges, so with zones the 3MF is for one slicer; all three fill the zone (slicer_check). 2026-10-31: strengthening holes not added — only forum sources, and slicers can treat the inner walls as outer walls (no infill between); the modifier zones already add walls/infill per region in all three slicers. Lighter zones are covered by the light base + strong zones. 2026-11-01 zone shapes: box, upright cylinder or ball inside the box (`Zone.shape`, `Zone.gap`), for picked zones and the advanced design boxes; a zone picked on an upright round hole or post is a cylinder round it by default; round modifier meshes in the 3MF; the strength check, hidden gaps and the keep-clear / gap notes use the shape; drawn round in the 3D view, "Shape" picker per zone and box.

- [x] **T-160 P3** (Phase 9) Simplified dynamic (vibration, impact) simulation. · owner: Claude (`task/T-160-dynamics`, UI in `task/T-142-ui-round2`) — `api.dynamics` (natural frequency as held, swing shape, impact factor 1 + √(1 + 2h/δ)), memory-safe subspace iteration (1–2 % from beam theory); Advanced → Will it hold? → Extra checks: shaking (mounted weight) and knocks (drop height), the part rocks in its swing shape (throttled, still with reduced motion).
- [x] **T-161 P3** (Phase 9) Heat in use (part temperature vs softening) and creep under a constant load. · owner: Claude (`task/T-161-heat-in-use`) — "Where is it used?" (room / warm spot / sun / car / motor) and "the load stays on": stiffness and strength scaled by the printed-PLA curve of Grasso et al. 2018 (modulus 1400/1143/961/350 MPa at 30/40/50/60 °C) relative to each material's `heat_limit_c` (approx. Tg, to be replaced by datasheets, G-3); `too_hot_in_use` finding near / past the limit; a constant load needs ×3 instead of ×2 (rule of thumb, no good creep data for prints yet). Other-material suggestions take the heat into account. Left: real creep data (time to failure / sag), heat limits from datasheets.
- [x] **T-162 P2** (Phase 9) Warping and residual stress with the deformation shown on the part; cooling settings optimised from the heat simulation. · owner: Claude (`task/T-162-warping`) — `warp.py`: inherent-strain simulation layer by layer (shrinkage from where the plastic sets down to the local temperature: warm near the bed, the printer's air higher up), bottom held by the bed; the base's curl after release rated against the longest bar that stays flat (thresholds / calibration print) on the same grid, so 1 = like that bar. Bars follow length (ABS open: 20 → 0.53, 45 → 1.0, 90 → 1.86), enclosures and PLA lower it, agrees with the length check on the samples; curled shape (exaggerated) and the first spots to lift in the view; ~20 s in the browser. 2026-10-31 cooling settings from the heat model: when small layers stay soft, the check re-runs the heat model with lower minimum print speeds (5/3/2 mm/s) and then longer layer times (15/20 s) and names the first that cools them (OrcaSlicer "Min print speed" / "Layer time"; the slicer's slow-down stops at the minimum speed — Orca wiki, Material cooling), or says to print two copies. 2026-10-31 the bed's warmth fades like heat along a cooling fin, sqrt(k·A/P/h) per voxel layer (thin wall ~1.7–1.9 mm, a 20 mm block much higher; more fan = shorter), replacing the 2 mm guess; **stress left inside** after cooling (element strain since it was printed minus its shrink, von Mises) as a second view of the warping result ("Show: the stress left inside") and one sentence against what the layers hold. G-code timing for warping → T-126.
- [~] **T-170 P2** (Phase 10) Calibration prints: tolerances / fits, overhang angle, bridges, support gap (layer strength and warping exist); presets shipped for printers that prove consistent. · owner: Claude (`task/T-170-calibration`) — three more test prints in My materials: fit gauge (6 mm peg, holes 6.0–6.5 mm) → snug / sliding gaps (`fit_gap_mm`, used by "What goes in it?"), overhang fan 40–70° → `max_overhang_deg`, bridges 10–50 mm → `max_bridge_mm`. Links to free community test prints (tolerance, stringing/bridging, Teaching Tech guide). Left: support Z-gap test, real prints to fill in shipped values.
- [x] **T-172 P1** Overhang check was more lenient than the material's angle. · owner: Claude (`task/T-172-overhang`) — two causes: each layer could reach half a line width past the angle (now 0.05 mm for slicing noise), and a strip along an edge narrower than the anchor margin counted as a short bridge "held all the way round" (now only pieces against the layer below on every side). PLA (55°) is now flagged from ~62° instead of not at all up to 75° on a leaning block; OrcaSlicer's default support threshold is 60°. Samples unchanged; regression test. Left: calibrate with the overhang test print (T-170).
- [x] **T-171 P3** (Phase 10) Material advisor from environment and load case. · owner: Claude (`task/T-171-material-advisor`) — "Which material suits it?": every material rated good / with care / not suitable with reasons: heat where it's used (`heat_limit_c`), sunlight (`uv_resistance`, approximate), flexible or not, what the printer can print (enclosure, hardened nozzle), and with loads set the safety factor; the easiest good one first. Strength's other-material options now include the heat too (they ignored it). API, MCP, web. Left: price, chemicals / food contact, datasheet values (G-3).

## User feedback round 1 (2026-10-31, from using the site)

Bugs first (P1), then UI clean-up, then new features.

### Bugs

- [x] **T-200 P1** Design changes that overlap give a rough model with mesh errors (two fills meeting at a corner). Union the edit pieces cleanly, or skip a piece that overlaps another. · owner: Claude (`task/T-200-edit-overlap`) — cause: cutters touched the part's own corner edges and end faces exactly (and per-edge wedges met at every corner of a curve), leaving zero-thickness fins and crumbs. Bottom chamfer is now one mitred sweep per outline loop with sections 0.01 mm off the corners; corner roundings one fan piece each, a hair past tangent; teardrops a hair past the hole; thicker-wall outlines simplified. All edits together on 19 samples/corpus parts × PLA/ABS: 38/38 clean (was 13/38). Loops too detailed for the sweep keep a plain edge (cable clip: slicer elephant-foot compensation instead).
- [x] **T-201 P1** GEOS error in the browser: `TopologyException: found non-noded intersection between LINESTRING (6.78023 19.9752, …) and LINESTRING (6.78023 19.9752, 6.78023 19.9752)` — a zero-length segment. Find which call (slicer / checks / design) and drop degenerate segments before GEOS sees them. · owner: Claude (`task/T-201-geos-zero-segment`) — reproduced with the user's dill holder after applying its design changes: the gap check subtracts a layer from its own closing, outlines almost on top of each other; Pyodide's GEOS 3.12.1 buffer leaves a 0.00002 mm segment and the plain overlay throws. `_safe_diff` now uses GEOS's robust overlay (grid_size 1e-4 mm) and keeps only the polygons. Finding counts unchanged natively; all samples, corpus parts and the edited part pass `pyodide_check`.
- [x] **T-202 P1** Section cut of the strength result looks broken (smeared colours, jagged stairs). Show clean coloured cubes in the cut. · owner: Claude (`task/T-201-bugs`) — with a cut, the part up to the cut is drawn as the solid analysis cubes (shaded), not the clipped hollow surface; displacement keeps the clipped surface (no values inside).
- [x] **T-203 P1** Nozzle comparison: a bigger nozzle can show a longer print time — check the time estimate (likely a bug). · owner: Claude (`task/T-203-nozzle-time`) — not a sum error: every nozzle got 3 walls and our PLA melt rate was too high. Walls and top/bottom keep the 0.4 mm plan's thickness with each nozzle's line width, per-nozzle speeds and OrcaSlicer's generic melt rates; within ×0.8–1.2 of OrcaSlicer at 0.4/0.6/0.8 mm. Bigger nozzles can really be slower on small parts (OrcaSlicer agrees); the table says why. (#11)
- [x] **T-204 P1** Results stay on screen when switching steps (warp shape after the warp simulation, loads in the design view, the "Report copied — paste it into…" note after leaving Download). Each step shows only its own things. · owner: Claude (`task/T-201-bugs`) — the 3D view draws per step: loads, stress, swing in Will it hold?; heat and warp in Problems; edit previews in Design changes; problem boxes in On the bed / Problems / Result. Expert follows the section scrolled to. A note from the last action is replaced on a step change.
- [x] **T-205 P1** "Show me" on a strength design suggestion runs but nothing visible happens (no camera move, no window). · owner: Claude (`task/T-201-bugs`) — opens Design changes (switches to Advanced if needed), suggests, lights up the chamfer at the weak spot in yellow and turns the view to it; says so when no chamfer fits.
- [x] **T-206 P1** "Download report" does not work. · owner: Claude (`task/T-201-bugs`) — there was no download, only "Copy report"; now "Download the report (with pictures)" in Download (all levels), see T-253.
- [x] **T-207 P1** "Held here?" box: the dropdown overflows the panel; wording "Holds here?" is clearer. · owner: Claude (`task/T-201-bugs`) — "Holds here?", shorter options, the dropdown gets its own line.
- [x] **T-268 P1** GEOS error in the browser again after a union: `TopologyException: found non-noded intersection between LINESTRING (13.5001 -0.548245, 18.5001 -0.548035) and LINESTRING (18.5001 -0.277007, 18.5001 -0.548035)` when the check runs on the STEP test part (teardrops applied) with a 6 mm "Add plastic" block joined on (T-251 e2e, Pyodide only; the engine then dies). Same family as T-201: find the call (likely another overlay that isn't `_safe_diff`) and make it robust. · owner: Claude (`task/T-268-geos-union`) — not a union: `_thick_blocks` (the "thick solid block" check) shrank each layer by 8 mm, and on an L-shaped layer 2–6 mm thick (L-bracket + teardrops + a block, rebuilt as 24 variants) Pyodide's GEOS 3.12.1 threw "depth mismatch" in that buffer (pip's GEOS doesn't). A first guard (drop pieces too thin to shrink) still crashed on a layer with a rounded micro-void, so the check no longer buffers at all: `checks._thick_core` rasterises the layer on a grid and finds the solid core with a distance transform (grid + distances, no overlay). Pyodide: all 25 crash variants and the samples pass; test `test_thick_blocks_without_a_buffer`.

### Engine

- [x] **T-230 P1** Orientation ranking: bed contact area matters only for warping materials (ABS, ASA, PA…), not for PLA. Penalise risky first layers: small islands, thin walls, points or sharp tips touching the bed; prefer a simple first layer. · owner: Claude (`task/T-230-first-layer`) — the footprint bonus now depends on the material's warp_risk (none for PLA/PETG/TPU, half for nylon, full for ABS/ASA/PC); the sliced refinement penalises small separate spots on the bed (< 25 mm², up to 3) and first layers made of thin strips (< 3 lines), with a plain reason. PLA L-bracket now prints on its side (lower, no supports), ABS keeps the base down
- [x] **T-231 P2** Nozzle comparison with the built-in slicer: detect details lost with a bigger nozzle (walls thinner than the line, small holes / text / gaps that vanish). · owner: Claude (`task/T-231-nozzle-detail`) — `checks.lost_detail`: per layer, what the nozzle's line can't trace (morphological opening at the thresholds' vanish / rough widths; corner slivers left out) → mm³ that won't print and that print rough, and the height losing most; problems a smaller nozzle doesn't have are marked "only with bigger nozzles". Test block fins: rough with 0.2, 71 mm³ lost with 0.4, 213 mm³ with 0.8; plain parts 0. Also in `tools/pyodide_check.mjs`.
- [x] **T-232 P1** Too weak with every print setting → say so and suggest design changes, a lower load or another material. · owner: Claude (`task/T-232-too-weak`) — when even the strongest settings miss the target, the optimiser returns `beyond` (worked out with those settings): the load it does hold (kg), clearly stronger materials, design changes at the weak spot; shown as "What else gets it to ×2" in the same boxes.
- [x] **T-233 P2** Slicer modifier regions in the UI: pick a region and give it its own settings (engine part exists from T-150 zones). · owner: Claude (`task/T-233-region-settings`) — "Stronger in one area" in Slicer settings (Advanced): pick a face (box around it + 3 mm) or, on a big face, the clicked spot (± 8 mm); set walls / infill (never below the rest) and how far it reaches; `api.zone_at` makes the same zones as the optimiser, so they go into the 3MF and the strength check; undo works. Lighter areas are left out: the strength model counts zones as never weaker.
- [x] **T-234 P2** Print time: OrcaSlicer gives the K1C ~3× shorter times than the P1S with near-identical settings (melt rate 14 vs 12 mm³/s, same speeds); find out why, then per-printer time constants. Our estimate is fitted to the P1S. · owner: Claude (task/T-234-orca-times) · Cause: OrcaSlicer's CLI ignores `inherits`, so the P1S got a 2 mm³/s melt rate; we now pass flattened profiles. Real P1S/K1C difference is ×1.11, so one set of constants (refit: typical ×1.11, worst ×1.16). Wall time now follows the wall area that fits (thin walls) and the melt-rate cap isn't scaled again; 0.4/0.6/0.8 mm nozzles within ×0.92–1.26 of OrcaSlicer.

### Simulations

- [x] **T-240 P2** Before a long simulation: estimate its time for this model and ask; let the user pick how much of the PC to use (threads / grid) and show the time for that choice. · owner: Claude (`task/T-220-versions`) — buttons show "about 48 s"; above 30 s a question first with the estimate ("Run it" / "Not now"). Estimates: one strength check takes about the same time on any part (the grid has ~12.5k cubes), measured on this computer and remembered; the others are multiples measured on the samples (fine grid 3.5×, warping 2.4×, shaking 3.5×, optimiser 4.5×), and the real time once run on this part. The choice offered is the work done (optimiser with or without stronger zones): the browser engine always runs on one core, so the PC stays usable; there is no thread count to pick. Native (MCP) runs keep the half-the-cores cap.
- [x] **T-241 P2** Faster shaking and knock checks (coarser grid, fewer modes, reuse the strength model). · owner: Claude (`task/T-241-faster-dynamics`) — about 2× faster (bar 4.6 → 2.3 s, mushroom 6.7 → 3.1 s natively, same Hz and safety): looser eigen-solver tolerances (inner 1e-3, frequency 0.1 %), only the ticked checks run, and the knock check reuses the last static solve (`api._evaluate`, also used by the stronger options and the material advisor). Fixed on the way: shaking / knocks used the goal's default walls and infill, not the ones set by hand or the zones. Coarser grid not needed.
- [x] **T-242 P3** Decide which extra simulations make sense as quick indicators: heat inside electronics boxes (fast), creep under a constant load, wear. Write down the yes/no with reasons, then build the yes ones. · owner: Claude (`task/T-242-extra-sims`) — decisions with sources in `research/extra_simulations.md`: electronics box heat yes (built: `api.box_heat`, ΔT = P / (U·A), U 4.6 W/m²K closed / 20 with a fan plus the printed wall, outside area as a prism; card in Optional simulations, verdict against the softening point and heat limit, one click hands the inside temperature to "Where is it used?"); creep no (×3 for constant loads already, no creep data per material); wear no (depends on the mating part, unreliable numbers).
- [x] **T-243 P2** Advanced: new step "Optional simulations" holding the extra simulations and the G-code (detailed) level. · owner: Claude (`task/T-220-versions`) — new Advanced step "Optional simulations" (after Will it hold?): warping, shaking and knocks (uses the held face and load from Will it hold?, says so until they are set), and the detailed check from a G-code. Their results show on the part only in this step.

### New sections

- [x] **T-250 P2** Advanced: "Tolerances & tuning" step — hole compensation moves here; the user marks surfaces that need a fit and what goes in (peg, bearing, screw…), we suggest the gap from the printer's standard value, the warp simulation and their own calibration prints (T-170 test prints live here). · owner: Claude (`task/T-220-versions`) — new Advanced step "Tolerances & tuning" (before Design changes): every round hole / post with "what goes in, how tight" (moved from Design changes; the size it works out is a design change, Design changes switches on by itself), the slicer's hole compensation and polyholes as fields (go into the 3MF, Undo works), and "Open the fit test" to tune the gaps to your printer. 2026-11-01 **slots and flat gaps**: "Pick two faces" (two parallel flat faces facing each other = slot, facing away = tab; `api.set_gap`), what goes in and how tight → the width to model (gap from the printer's standard or the fit test; faces lying flat: across the layers, + the sideways extra, at least one layer), offered as a design change that moves both faces by half (a slab of the face's outline added or cut). **Gaps from the warp simulation**: after a warping run for this orientation, how much a slot narrows / a tab thickens (faces' relative movement) or a hole squeezes / a post grows (wall movement toward the axis, both sides) is added to the gap and said in the explanation (rough).
- [x] **T-251 P2** Advanced: "Advanced design changes" step (simple chamfers / holes stay in Design): custom supports, extra faces, internal voids for extra walls, keep-clear volumes between picked points or faces, import other models and snap them into place (assemblies) — input for strength optimisation and AI design. · owner: Claude (`task/T-251-advanced-design`); the rest GPT-6.2 Sol, reviewed by Claude (`task/T-251-slicer-check`) — new Advanced step "Advanced design changes": boxes placed on a face, a spot or between two spots (`api.volume_at`): **Support here** / **No support here** (support enforcer / blocker in the 3MF for OrcaSlicer, Bambu Studio and PrusaSlicer; with supports off, only the placed ones are printed: `tree(manual)` / `support_material_auto=0`; the blocker warns when it leaves an overhang in the air), **Hidden gap** (a negative volume inside the part; one click makes a 0.6 mm pin hole up through the part with 1 mm cover, two clicks a slot; the slicer prints walls round it, the strength check counts it as solid walls around an empty box; warns when it reaches the surface), **Keep clear** (not exported; says how much of the part is in it). Colours in the 3D view, notes per box, resize, Undo. 2026-11-01 **Add plastic / Cut away** blocks (same picking and sizing, shown in their own colours; "Apply to the model" joins or cuts them as a new version, `api.volume_solid`; not in the 3MF); **Add another model**: an STL / 3MF whose biggest flat face goes flat onto the clicked face, centred there, turned by a set angle, joined as a new version (`api.snap_model`); **keep-clear** boxes go to the design suggestions: changes that would add plastic into one come back with `clash` and stay unticked. `tools/slicer_check.py` verified the G-code in OrcaSlicer 2.5.1, Bambu Studio 1.8.2 and PrusaSlicer 2.8.0: the enforcer added support under only the selected overhang with automatic supports off, the blocker removed support under that overhang and kept it under the other, and the hidden gap had walls around it and no extrusion in its centre. OrcaSlicer and PrusaSlicer widened the support foot slightly past the enforcer box near the bed.
- [x] **T-252 P2** Expert: same steps as Advanced, plus every value changeable: mesh / grid size, solver settings, detection thresholds (wall thickness…), all research-based defaults from `thresholds.yaml` — in a separate settings window split by section, nothing hidden. · owner: Claude (`task/T-252-expert-settings`) — "All settings" window (Expert, top bar): the analysis grids (strength / fine / warping cubes) and all 136 numbers in `thresholds.yaml`, 18 sections, each with its default and the note from the file (what it does, its research source); filter, changed values marked, reset. Saved in the browser, sent again after an engine restart, the checks run again on close. Engine: `profile.set_expert` (overrides on top of the data files), `api.expert_settings` / `api.set_expert`. Left: solver settings (tolerances) and the orientation score weights aren't in it yet.
- [x] **T-253 P2** Downloadable report for people: images for each suggestion and the simulation results, readable on its own. · owner: Claude (`task/T-201-bugs`) — one HTML file (opens anywhere, prints to PDF): verdict, the part on the bed, one card per kind of problem with a close-up picture, strength result with loads and colours, warp shape, chosen design changes, settings table with reasons. Pictures are taken from the 3D view with only that section's things drawn; the camera goes back after. ~300 kB for the L-bracket with 7 pictures.

## User feedback round 2 (2026-11-01, from using the site)

Order: bugs (P1) first, then the wrong strength advice (P1), then the smarter "Make it stronger" (T-280 … T-284),
then simulations and UI, then the bigger extras (topology optimisation).

### Bugs


- [x] **T-270 P1** Picking a face on a rounded part selects the whole side: on an extruded profile with rounded corners (dog-bone / H shape, STL) one click on the side wall turns the entire wall around the part green, so a held face or load can't be put on one side. The region grows across the smooth fillets because each step only compares neighbours. Split it: a face must also stay within ~30–40° of the normal where it was clicked (or stop at clearly curved bands), so flat sides and the round corners between them become separate faces. Keep CAD (STEP) faces as they are. Shift-click to add a neighbouring face if the user wants more. Test: the dog-bone sample gives one face per flat side. · files: `core/fdmcore/regions.py` (`find_regions`), `web/app.js` (picking) · owner: Claude (`task/T-270-face-split`) — cause: a flat side of two triangles counted as a "small strip", so the fillets on either side pulled it into one face all round the outline. Smooth strips now only join neighbours of about their own width (≤ 3×, or both under 1 mm, so a thin seam strip stays in its hole) that bend the same way (out or in): the rounded dog-bone gives 24 wall faces (12 sides + 12 corners) instead of 1, hole walls stay one face (`core/tests/test_regions.py`). Shift-click not needed: several held faces / loads can already be added one by one.
- [x] **T-271 P1** Overlapping fillets / chamfers from the design suggestions leave small loose pieces after "Apply" (a little wedge floating next to the corner, see the dog-bone with a chamfer at the waist). After the union / cut, drop pieces that are tiny or not joined to the part, and when two suggested edits overlap, merge them or skip the smaller one before applying (follow-up of T-200). Test: two chamfers meeting at a corner give one closed piece. · files: `core/fdmcore/features.py`, `web/geometry.js` · owner: Claude (`task/T-271-edit-slivers`) — after the booleans, every piece that doesn't overlap the original part is dropped (browser `web/geometry.js` and local `booleans.py`; a piece a cut splits off still overlaps and stays); the thicker weak spot no longer makes slabs cut off from the part by the round around the spot; `concave_edges` skips sliver triangles whose corners round to one point (NaN directions on applied versions). Your exact shape didn't reproduce on a rounded dog-bone (one and two rounds of changes); if a wedge still stays attached, send the STL. Seen meanwhile: thickening a flat part also grows below the bed — noted in T-281.
- [x] **T-272 P1** Optional simulations → Warping: the result box breaks the layout (the green box sticks out over the right edge of its card, the "Show … the curled shape" dropdown is too wide). Keep it inside the card; dropdown on its own line or shorter option text (like T-207). · files: `web/app.js` (`warp-show`), `web/style.css` · owner: Claude (`task/T-272-warp-layout`) — "Show it on the part" checkbox, then the dropdown on its own line, full width of the box, shorter options ("Curled shape (red dots lift first)" / "Stress left inside after cooling"); controls in any result box wrap instead of pushing it wider. Checked in Edge: the box stays inside its card.
- [x] **T-273 P1** Clicking "What can you change?" (mode) jumps back to the strength step. Changing the mode re-runs the check but must leave the user on the step they're on. · files: `web/app.js` (mode `onchange`, `runCheck`), `web/steps.js` · owner: Claude (`task/T-273-keep-step`) — the jump came from clicking **the part** in the 3D view: every click picked a face and opened "Will it hold?" (where the hold / load panel is). Now only on that step; elsewhere the face is still picked (waiting there) and the status line says where to use it. The "What can you change?" options and the level switch were checked too: they keep the step.
- [x] **T-274 P1** The detailed strength simulation shows nothing while it runs. Show the working badge with what it's doing ("Building the grid…", "Solving 2 of 3 loads…") and the time left, like the other long runs. · files: `web/app.js`, `web/worker.js` · owner: Claude (`task/T-274-detailed-feedback`) — the run itself had the working badge; what looked like nothing: the G-code card is in Optional simulations but its result only showed in "Will it hold?", and with no held face / load yet, loading the G-code or ticking "Use it" did nothing at all. The card now says what's next ("tap where it's held and where the load sits", button to open the step) or shows the result ("With your sliced print: safety ×7.1", button); the working text says "Simulating the load with your sliced print (its walls, infill and line directions) (about 12 s)". e2e checks the card result.

### Strength advice that is wrong

- [x] **T-275 P1** "Make it stronger" suggests PLA-CF: carbon-fibre filled filaments are **stiffer, not stronger** — tensile strength and impact are about the same or lower than the plain plastic and layers bond worse. Check the material data (presets and user presets / `based_on` copies must not carry a higher strength for CF / GF types), and never offer a filled material under "stronger" unless its own data says it is; offer it only as "bends less" when the part is too bendy. Sources for the numbers in the PR (datasheets: Bambu PLA-CF / PLA Basic, Prusament PLA / PETG-CF). Add a rule in `rules.yaml`. · files: `core/fdmcore/api.py` (`strength_options`, material loop), `core/fdmcore/data/presets.yaml`, `web/mypresets.js` · owner: Claude (`task/T-275-cf-not-stronger`) — fibre filled filament (built-in `fibre: carbon` on PA-CF, a new `fibre` value for your own materials, or -CF / -GF / "carbon fibre" in the name; not "polycarbonate") is never offered under "stronger", whatever its numbers; the material advisor says "carbon fibre: stiffer, not stronger: more brittle and weaker between the layers"; new rule `fibre_filled` (Polymaker PLA-CF vs PLA TDS, Bambu ASA-CF guide, CNC Kitchen). Orca import of PLA-CF / PETG-CF / ABS-GF starts from the plain plastic with ×0.67 strength and ×0.75 layer strength (Polymaker TDS) instead of plain PLA's numbers; PA6 / PA12 / PAHT(-CF) map to PA-CF. Left: offering it as "bends less" needs a stiffness check first (none yet).
- [x] **T-276 P1** "Carry less" is never the first answer to "How can I make it stronger?": it is always the last group, after settings, orientation, design and material — even when it has the best number. · files: `web/app.js` (`OPTION_GROUP` order / which box starts open), `core/fdmcore/api.py` · owner: Claude (`task/T-276-load-last`) — the engine sorts strongest first with "Carry less" always last (`api._strongest_first`, also for the ideas after the lightest-settings search), so its box never starts open; test: an overloaded bar where carrying less has the best number still lists it last.
- [x] **T-277 P2** "Strong in one area" zones from the strength simulation: when a strength result exists, propose the zones (more walls / denser infill) where the stress is high, instead of a guess; the user can still move or remove them. · files: `core/fdmcore/api.py` (zones), `web/app.js` · owner: Claude (`task/T-277-zones-from-stress`) — the strength check now returns `stress_zones` (`api._stress_zones`: the optimiser's `find_zones` boxes round the most stressed cubes, two more walls and ≥ 40 % infill, with how hard each works vs. the worst spot). "Stronger in one area" offers them in one click ("Make them stronger areas"), listed as "Stressed place 1 …", editable / removable / undoable like picked ones. Test `test_strength_proposes_zones_where_the_stress_is`; e2e in "stronger area picked on the part".


### Smarter "Make it stronger" (design changes)

- [x] **T-280 P2** Chamfers at every weak inside corner, not only the worst one: suggest a chamfer at each inside corner whose stress is above ~60–70 % of the highest, so one round of changes helps instead of moving the weak spot to the next corner and needing many re-runs. · files: `core/fdmcore/features.py` (`_stress_chamfers`), `core/fdmcore/api.py` · owner: Claude (`task/T-280-all-weak-corners`) — the strength check keeps every spot with at least 65 % of the highest stress (`Session.hot_spots`, not at supports / loads); the stress chamfer covers the worst inside corner plus every other inside corner within 3 analysis cubes of such a spot, highest first, up to 4, as one change ("2.0 mm 45° chamfers at the 2 inside corners with the most stress"), so "Test it" fixes them in one go. Test part, two equal posts pushed sideways: worst corner only 2.07 → 2.04 (the weak spot just moved), both 2.07 → 2.85 (`test_chamfers_at_every_weak_corner`).
- [x] **T-281 P2** Thicken only where it is weak: (also: today it grows below the bed on a part lying flat — keep the bed face flat.) "Make the weak spot thicker" grows plastic by the stress field (where stress is above a share of the highest, tapering out), not all round. For the dog-bone, the narrow middle gets thicker, the wide ends don't. Compare against all-round thickening on the test parts. · files: `core/fdmcore/features.py`, `core/fdmcore/api.py` · owner: Claude (`task/T-281-thicken-where-weak`) — full thickness along the stretch that is nearly as stressed as the weak spot (the hot spots of T-280; all of them when they run along the member: a neck in tension peaks where it meets the wide ends), only on sections up to 1.5× the narrowest one there; beyond it the taper keeps the neck's shape and sections already bigger (the wide ends) get nothing; nothing below the bed (clipped at the bed plane). The member direction is the narrowest of: the solid nearby, the hot spots' long direction, the axis of a disc of them (a stem in tension) — the worst spot sits where the neck meets the wide part, and the solid there pointed across it. Results with the app's own load carry-over: flat dog-bone 1.33 → 1.48 with the ends untouched (was 0.09: ends widened, grew 1.5 mm below the bed), mushroom 1.73 → 1.90 with 714 mm³ (was 1.72 with 7830), after the chamfer 1.92 → 2.83 (browser e2e: 1.9 → 2.8, was 1.9 → 1.9). Builds on T-280 (and T-271): merge after them.
- [x] **T-282 P2** Rules: chamfer or fillet for strength, depending on print direction. Research and write down (with sources) when a fillet is better (spreads stress best, fine on edges that stand up on the bed) and when a 45° chamfer is better (edges facing down that would need support, first-layer edges, elephant's foot). The suggestion picks by orientation and says why. Entry in `rules.yaml`. · files: `core/fdmcore/data/rules.yaml`, `core/fdmcore/features.py`, `research/` · owner: Claude (`task/T-282-round-or-chamfer`) — rule (rules.yaml `stress_at_inside_corner`, sources added: ETASR 2025 FEA — fillets give the lowest stress concentration; Rahix / Slant 3D — rounds hang over, 45° chamfers support themselves; bed edges were already chamfer-only): each weak inside corner gets a **round** unless the round's surface would face down more than the material's overhang limit somewhere along it (a corner under a ledge / ceiling), then a **45° chamfer**. The round is as wide as gives the same plastic as the chamfer (×1.53): just as wide it fills less than half and checked weaker (mushroom 1.78 vs 1.92); with the same plastic both check the same (1.90 vs 1.92) — our 1 mm cubes see plastic, not the smoothness that makes rounds better. Mushroom cap down: "3.1 mm round", cap up: "2.0 mm 45° chamfer" (`test_round_where_it_prints_chamfer_where_it_would_hang`). UI says "the rounded corners". Gemini timed out twice, research done by hand.
- [x] **T-283 P2** Engine: test design variants in the background. `api.try_designs`: builds variants (chamfer / fillet sizes e.g. 1 / 2 / 4 mm, how many weak corners get one, thickening amount and area), runs the strength check on each (coarse grid first, the best ones again on the normal grid), returns the measured safety, weight and print-time change per variant. Progress per step for the UI. Low priority / capped threads so the PC stays usable. · files: `core/fdmcore/api.py`, `core/fdmcore/features.py` · owner: Claude (`task/T-283-try-designs`) — the booleans that build a variant run in the browser (manifold-3d), not in the engine, so: engine `api.strength_variant(up, loads, data)` checks a changed copy without replacing the part (held faces / loads move to the matching face like a new version does; returns safety, verdict, `added_g`); the web app builds the variants (rounded corners 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 mm, thicker weak spot 1 / 1.5 / 2.5 mm, then the best of each together; or 2 / 4 and 1.5 for the quicker choice) and checks each — see T-284. Also fixed on the way: the thickening could take a wide part as the narrow member when the weak stretch lay just inside it (+34 cm³ on the mushroom); the narrowest section is now looked for 8 mm either side of the stretch too.
- [x] **T-284 P2** UI: "Make it stronger" has **Quick** (as now: no design simulation) and **Thorough** (uses T-283). Thorough only shows when the user said they can change the design (mode). The variants are not shown as model versions while testing; the result lists real numbers ("2 mm chamfers at 3 corners: safety 1.4 → 2.3, +2 g") and only the one the user picks becomes a new version. · files: `web/app.js`, `web/worker.js` · owner: Claude (`task/T-283-try-designs`) — "How can I make it stronger?" stays the quick one; new "Test design changes for me" next to it (only when the mode lets the design change), with the time estimate first and a choice of every size (8 checks) or a few (4). The list shows measured numbers ("×2.3 (+35 %) · +1.2 g of plastic"), tags "strongest" and "enough, least plastic"; nothing becomes a model version until "Use this". e2e step "thorough: test design changes in the background". - [x] **T-291 P3** Real topology optimisation (opt-in, only when the design can change): SIMP on the voxel strength grid (numpy / scipy only, coarse grid, capped threads), keeps loads, held faces and keep-clear boxes; result shown as a suggested lighter shape and as an STL to rebuild in CAD. Time estimate first; first a research note on what runs in the browser in reasonable time. · files: new `core/fdmcore/topopt.py` (+ `make_manifest.py`), `web/app.js` · owner: Claude (`task/T-291-topopt`) — research note done: `research/topology_optimisation.md`. SIMP (p 3, density filter, OC update) on the strength grid fits: one solve ~1 s at 3 000 cubes and 3–4 s at 12 000 in Pyodide while the density is smooth, so 40 iterations take ~1 / ~2.5 min; but near-empty voxels (contrast 1000:1) slow CG 10–30× (measured), so use ρ_min 10⁻², warm-started CG and dropping empty voxels. 2026-11-07 phase 2: `topopt.py` (SIMP p 3, ρ_min 10⁻², density filter 1.5 cubes, OC update, warm-started CG with a looser tolerance in the first half; cubes at loads and held faces stay; only removes plastic; loose bits dropped), `api.topology` (Quick 3 000 / Fine 12 000 cubes, keep 15–90 %) → blocky STL, grams vs. the part, safety of both printed solid on the same grid. Cantilever test: plastic goes to the top and bottom edges (truss), volume as asked; 3 700 cubes in ~12 s natively. Web: "A lighter shape" in Design changes (Advanced, keep slider 20–80 %, Quick / Fine asked like other long runs, shown as a new version, STL download); MCP `suggest_lighter_shape`, `fdmdfm topology`. Left: smoother shapes (marching cubes) and a check on real redesigned parts.

## Audit (2026-11-08, Claude + Gemini 4 Argon, read-only)

Bugs and hardening found in the audit (each checked in the code); file:line as of `4cfaa85`.

### Bugs

- [x] **T-300 P1** Engine state gets lost or out of step with the screen: (a) **Cancel** restarts the worker and replays only presets, expert values, load, body, scale and G-code; the hole / post answers (`tag_feature`), fits (`set_fit`), slot / tab gaps (`set_gap`), the strength weak spot / hot spots and the warp result are gone, while the UI still shows them, so later checks quietly ignore them (`web/app.js:62-81`, `api.py` `Session`). (b) **Opening a project** runs the first check (`selectOption(0)`) before marks and slicer overrides are restored, and `constant_load` is saved but never restored (`app.js:270-290`, `app.js:140`). (c) **Switching back to a version** (Model list / Undo) loses the unit rescale (×25.4 / ×1000): `showVersion` never re-sends `scale` (`app.js:2417-2426`). Same root cause: keep one serialisable session object (tags, fits, gaps, scale, marks, overrides, constant load) and replay it the same way on cancel, version switch and project open; clear weak spot / warp results the UI shows when they can't be restored. Add an e2e check that Cancel keeps a fit and a hole answer. · files: `web/app.js`, `web/project.js`, `core/fdmcore/api.py` · owner: Claude (task/T-300-session-state) → The app keeps the engine's `session_state` (hole / post answers, fits, gaps, weak spot, hot spots) after each call that changes it and gives it back with `restore_state` after a Cancel; the warp result is cleared (re-run). Projects: marks and slicer settings before the first check, constant load restored, the status waits for the nozzle comparison. Versions keep their body and unit rescale. New pytest and e2e checks (Cancel keeps a fit and a hole answer).
- [x] **T-301 P1** "Too big for your printer" (`exceeds_build_volume`, built by hand in `api.check`, `api.py:2073`) and the G-code heat spot (`_spot_findings`, `api.py:2128`) have no `impact`, so `emphasize_for_goal` sorts them as info / "Other" instead of "Can fail" / shape. - [x] **T-302 P1** Warping simulation on tilted orientations: `warp.simulate` voxelises in part coordinates and takes the bed as nodes within 1e-6 of the lowest height along `up` (`warp.py:65-77`); for an `up` that isn't along an axis only a corner or edge of the stair-stepped grid touches, so the part is barely held and the curl is wrong. Rotate the mesh into the print frame before voxelising (rotate the result back), add a 45° test in `test_warp.py`. · files: `core/fdmcore/warp.py`, `core/tests/test_warp.py` · owner: Claude (task/T-301-engine-bugs, with T-302, T-303, T-306, T-307: one PR, one CI run) → The part is voxelised in the print frame (`rotation` in the result, callers map points back); a 30° tilted bar curls like the flat one.

### Security and robustness

- [x] **T-310 P1** Size limits for files that are read: 3MF / `.gcode.3mf` entries read without a size check (zip bomb, `formats.py:67-134`, `api.py:1289`); 3MF components that reference the same object many times multiply (depth cap 16, no total cap); one `G2`/`G3` line with a huge radius asks for ~10⁹ points (`gcode.py:194`, cap the segment count); very big STLs / project files. Plain messages ("this file unpacks to more than … MB"). Matters most for the MCP server (crashes the local process); in the browser it only kills the tab. · files: `core/fdmcore/formats.py`, `core/fdmcore/gcode.py`, `core/fdmcore/mesh.py`, `core/fdmcore/api.py` · owner: Claude (task/T-310-limits) · Done (branch task/T-310-limits, bundled with T-311/T-314/T-316 for one CI run): zip entries over 400 MB refused (header size can't lie past the limit), 3MF triangles counted before unpacking (component bombs caught), 4 M triangle cap for STL/3MF, G2/G3 arcs capped at 720 points; tests in test_formats.
- [x] **T-311 P1** Service worker: old caches are never deleted, so every Pyodide / library version bump leaves ~100 MB+ behind (`web/sw.js`, delete caches not named in the current version on `activate`). On Pages the libraries are same-origin under `/vendor/` and go through network-first with a revalidation request each; serve them cache-first (versioned paths). · files: `web/sw.js` · owner: Claude (task/T-310-limits) · Done (task/T-310-limits): activate deletes caches with other names; fetching a new library version drops the old version's same files; same-origin /vendor/ is cache-first. e2e offline step passes.
- [x] **T-312 P2** Supply chain: `tools/vendor.py` downloads ~117 MB without checking hashes, and the Pages cache keys only on `vendor.py`; the browser imports have no Subresource Integrity, no Content-Security-Policy. Pin sha256 per vendored file, add `integrity` where the loader allows it, and a CSP (`script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval' https://cdn.jsdelivr.net; object-src 'none'`), checked with the e2e test. · files: `tools/vendor.py`, `web/index.html`, `.github/workflows/pages.yml` · owner: Claude (task/T-312-supply-chain) · Done (task/T-312-supply-chain): vendor.py checks every file against a pinned sha256 (Pyodide packages against the pinned lock's sha256), also files from the cache, and stops on a mismatch (`--hashes` prints new lines after a version bump); the import map carries `integrity` for three.js, manifold and OpenCascade, the STEP reader's script tag too; CSP meta (`script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval' 'unsafe-eval' cdn + import-map hashes; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'`), Pages drops the CDN from it; test_web_csp checks both import-map hashes; e2e 50/50 with it. 'unsafe-eval' is needed by OpenCascade → T-327.
- [x] **T-313 P2** MCP server reads and writes any path the agent passes, and overwrites without asking (`mcp_server.py`, `reports.py:172`, `charts.py:168`). Optional allowed folder (flag / env var, default: no limit as today, documented), `overwrite=False` by default for outputs, expected file extensions. `save_project` can't save marks, fits, gaps, tags, constant load (follows T-300). · files: `core/fdmcore/mcp_server.py` · owner: Claude (task/T-313-mcp-viewer-speed) · Done (task/T-313-mcp-viewer-speed, bundled with T-315/T-317 for one CI run): `--folder` / `FDM_DFM_FOLDER` keeps the MCP server in one folder (default: no limit, as before, documented in README); reads and writes check the file type; it never replaces an existing file it didn't write itself this session (`FDM_DFM_OVERWRITE=1` allows it; chosen over an `overwrite` argument on ten tools so the agent's save-again loop keeps working). Left: `save_project` with marks/fits/gaps/tags needs T-300's `session_state` merged first.
- [x] **T-314 P3** Report escaping: Markdown tables don't escape `|` / newlines (`reports.py:166`, `api.py:2359`); the HTML `esc()` skips `'` (`web/report.js:4`, harmless today). · files: `core/fdmcore/reports.py`, `web/report.js` · owner: Claude (task/T-310-limits) · Done (task/T-310-limits): `api.md_cell` escapes `|` and line breaks in both Markdown tables; report.js `esc` also escapes `'`.
- [x] **T-315 P2** Memory over a long session: `viewer.js` removes the strength colour mesh, the section cubes and the overlay groups without `dispose()` of geometry / material (`viewer.js:275-429`); every model version keeps a full STL copy with no limit (`state.versions`). · files: `web/viewer.js`, `web/app.js` · owner: Claude (task/T-313-mcp-viewer-speed) · Done (task/T-313-mcp-viewer-speed): viewer `dispose()` frees geometry, materials and textures of the markers, symbols, zones, ghosts, bed, strength colours and section cubes; model versions past 300 MB let go of their model, oldest first (results stay in the table, marked "results only").
- [x] **T-316 P2** GEOS safety: `refine.py:46-62` runs plain `difference` / `union` / `intersection` on layer outlines (`_safe_diff` is used only at line 85); route them through the robust helpers and run `node tools/pyodide_check.mjs` on the samples and corpus. · files: `core/fdmcore/refine.py`, `core/fdmcore/checks.py` · owner: Claude (task/T-310-limits) · Done (task/T-310-limits): refine.py difference/union/intersection go through `checks._safe_overlay` (the robust grid overlay `_safe_diff` uses); pyodide_check passes on the samples.
- [x] **T-317 P2** Speed on big parts (follows T-004): `features.concave_edges` loops over every half-edge in Python (`features.py:785-809`, reuse the vectorised matcher in `regions.face_adjacency`), `gcode.parse` builds millions of Python tuples (`gcode.py:101-159`). · files: `core/fdmcore/features.py`, `core/fdmcore/gcode.py` · owner: Claude (task/T-313-mcp-viewer-speed) · Done (task/T-313-mcp-viewer-speed): `concave_edges` vectorised (same output on all 19 sample/corpus parts, ~10× faster); `gcode.parse` collects moves as raw doubles instead of a list of tuples (~10× less memory).

### New features

- [x] **T-320 P2** Screws, nuts and heat-set inserts (continues T-023): for a hole the user picks (or one we detect), ask what goes in and size it for them: **screw straight into the plastic** (e.g. M3: pilot hole per material and nozzle, thread-forming screws, how deep, enough walls / wall thickness round it), **heat-set insert** (the insert's hole diameter and depth for M2–M5 common inserts, wall round it, a lead-in chamfer, which way up it prints best), **nut** (hex pocket from the top, or a sideways slot for a nut pushed in from the side; captive pocket with a pause, see T-322), **bolt through** (clearance hole, washer face). Left from T-023: sideways nut slots, big hex pockets in non-vertical orientations. Gives the hole size (pilot / insert / ISO 273 clearance, with the fit-test gap and the sideways allowance for bolts), depth (2× d for screws; insert length + 0.5 mm, Ruthex), measured wall (cut across the hole) vs. what it needs, the walls to print when it can't change, a washer face for bolts. Flat cuts across the part's longest axis that fits in n pieces (2–6), each cut nudged up to a quarter piece onto a one-piece section and away from the last strength check's weak spot; each piece = the part with the rest cut away (cut edits for manifold-3d / booleans.py); Ø4 mm pins (two per cut, far apart, 2 mm wall), holes with the snug fit gap (fit test print if measured), glue only when the face is too thin. Follow-up (task/T-321b-dovetail-bed): `joint="dovetail"` (app: "Join the pieces with"; MCP `split_part(joint=...)`): a sliding dovetail per cut (15° flare, runs along the cut face's shorter side, groove wider by the snug gap; pins when the face has no room or the tongue would make a piece too long for the bed). "Too big" now shows the pieces on the bed first, one plate each, turned the way they print (`rotation` per piece: lying lowest, dovetail upright), then downloads them. Fixed: pieces cut across y got no pin holes (the hole solids were inside out). Left: cuts that aren't across the part's own axes.
- [x] **T-322 P2** Pause for parts put in while printing (magnets, nuts, bearings, weights): find closed pockets with a flat roof, tell the layer number / height to pause at, warn against supports inside the pocket and when the roof is a long bridge; add the pause to the 3MF where the slicer supports it (Orca / Bambu `layer_gcode` / pause at layer, Prusa `M601`), checked with `tools/slicer_check.py`. · files: `core/fdmcore/checks.py`, `core/fdmcore/export3mf.py`, `rules.yaml` · owner: Claude (task/T-322-pause) · Done (task/T-322-pause): closed pockets (inner shells) with a flat roof give `pause_to_insert` (layer number and height, warns when the roof is longer than the material bridges; slicer fix: supports on the build plate only). Download has "Pause to put parts into the pocket"; `export(..., pauses=[pocket tops])` writes OrcaSlicer's custom_gcode_per_layer.xml and PrusaSlicer's custom_gcode_per_print_z.xml (type 1 = pause, the printer profile's pause command). Orca rounds to the nearest layer, Prusa to the next one up, so each gets its own height; slicer_check.py `pause_at_layer` passes for both. Bambu Studio ignores the pause in a 3MF it didn't write (checked): the finding tells the user to add it on that layer. Follow-up (task/T-322b-bambu-pause): a 3MF that claims to be a Bambu project makes Bambu Studio 1.8 crash without the whole printer setup, so the pause goes into the sliced file instead: Download has "Add the pause to a file sliced in Bambu Studio" (`api.pause_gcode`, MCP `add_pause_to_gcode`), plain G-code or `.gcode.3mf` (its .md5 updated); the printer's own pause command from the file's settings (Bambu M400 U1 when empty, Prusa M601), after the move up to the first layer over the pocket. slicer_check.py `pause_at_layer` now passes for Bambu Studio too.
- [x] **T-323 P2** Modelled threads (M3–M6 bolts and nuts in the model, standing or lying): 60° flanks and crests finer than the layer height print rough or seize; detect them and suggest a finer / variable layer height, standing the thread up, a larger clearance, or using a heat-set insert / tap instead (links to T-320). · files: `core/fdmcore/features.py`, `rules.yaml` · owner: Claude (task/T-323-threads) · Done (task/T-323-threads): `features.find_threads` finds threads along the model's axes (60° flanks: normals ~30° off the axis, climbing one ISO 261 coarse pitch per turn, right or left hand), with size, pitch, major/minor Ø, bolt or hole; `modelled_thread` per thread: below M5 (Hubs), fewer than 4 layers per pitch standing up (finer / variable layer height), lying on its side, 0.3 mm clearance (Prusa KB) and cleaning the thread. Tested on generated M3 / M6 / M10 and fine M6 threads; no hits on the samples and corpus.
- [x] **T-324 P2** Cost and spool: price per kg on your materials (My materials), cost per orientation / setting including support; "grams left on the spool" warns when this print (with support and a margin) won't finish it. · files: `web/mypresets.js`, `core/fdmcore/checks.py`, `web/app.js` · owner: Claude (task/T-324-cost-spool) · Done (task/T-324-cost-spool): My materials has "Price per kg" and "Grams left on the spool" (engine fields `price_per_kg`, `spool_left_g`); check stats give `support_g` (refine.support_stats volume × 0.25 fill, rough), `total_g`, `cost`, and `spool_short` when total × 1.1 + 5 g is more than what's left; the stats line, the report and the version table show them.   - Done 2026-11-20 (Gemini 4 Neon, reviewed by Claude): `snap_fit_upright` now reports the tab's bending strain (ε = 1.5·t·y/L², Bayer snap-fit guide; Loftsmith) against the material's allowed strain, halved when the tab bends across its layers (Fictiv, Loftsmith), and gives the CAD numbers: minimum length, maximum thickness or maximum hook. Allowed strain is 70% of the printed plastic's yield strain (Bayer): PLA 2.0 %, PETG 3.5 %, ABS 1.5 %, ASA 2.4 %, PC 4.0 % (Prusament / Stratasys data sheets). PLA+, CF nylon and TPU have no sourced limit: strain only, no limit or advice. Only `cable_clip` (lying on its side) has a snap among the sample parts: PLA, 2.0 % vs 1 % allowed → "at least 18.2 mm long, at most 1.33 mm thick, or hook at most 0.42 mm".

### Bugs

### Print failure risk

- [x] **T-390 P2** Bed adhesion: estimate whether warping can pull an edge off the bed. · files: `core/fdmcore/warp.py`, `core/fdmcore/data/thresholds.yaml`, `rules.yaml` · owner: GPT-6.2 Sol, reviewed by Claude (`task/T-390-bed-adhesion`) — Added a short edge-peel solve and editable low-confidence grip estimates. The 40 mm open-printer ABS bar holds and the 90 mm bar peels in native Python and Pyodide. The local finding contributes to print risk and suggests ways to hold the part down. Validate grip and peel against real prints alongside T-477.
- [x] **T-391 P2** Print failure risk rating from the printer (enclosure, bed) and the warping / adhesion simulation, with a brim or raft suggested from it. · files: `core/fdmcore/api.py`, `core/fdmcore/data/rules.yaml` · owner: Claude (`task/T-391-print-risk`) — Done 2026-11-20: `recommend.print_risk` adds up what the check finds (corners that lift, a small base, a tall narrow part, ABS / ASA / PC over 50 mm on an open printer) and the last warping simulation's verdict for this orientation, material and enclosure, into low / medium / high with plain reasons. `check` returns it as `print_risk`; high becomes the finding `print_may_fail` (impact fails, rules.yaml with brim / raft / bed fixes). The brim choice now comes from it, plus a raft as the last resort (a tiny base that also warps or tips, or the simulation says ABS-like plastic lifts on an open printer): `raft_layers` 3, no brim, no elephant foot compensation, in the 3MF for Orca / Bambu / PrusaSlicer and named for Cura. Not done: whether the warping pull beats the bed's grip (needs T-390's peeling model and measured grip values).

## Audit round 4 (2026-11-15, Claude: usability, scalability, user needs, design)

A read-only audit of the web tool. Everything a task needs is written in the task; no chat is needed.

**How it was checked** (so the "Seen" lines can be repeated):
- Headless Edge (Playwright, 1440 × 900 and a 390 × 844 phone size), walking Simple, Basic and Advanced with the sample parts.
- The tools run for real on `l_bracket`: strength (5 kg on one face, another face held), lightest settings, shaking and knocks, warping (PLA on the P1S, ABS on the A1), design changes applied, 3MF and report downloaded, Cancel during a run.
- Three generated organic parts (a bumpy blob, a stand-in for a scan or sculpt) with 80,000 / 320,000 / 980,000 triangles.
- Natively: every suggested design change applied one by one and all together on the 10 sample parts, the 10 corpus parts and the 80,000-triangle blob (112 changes), and the slicer settings of those 21 parts compared (PLA, Bambu Lab P1S, 0.4 mm, Balanced).
- Not checked: a real phone, a real slicer opening the 3MF, a real print, STEP files, G-code import, the fit tools, autosave / restore, offline use, slow connections.
- Baseline: the audit ran on `main` at `0babeec` (2026-11-14). After it, T-369 (guided strength flow, the step is now called "Strength analysis"), T-370 (brush), T-382 (less text: explanations behind an ⓘ, "Explain") and T-392 (boxes moved and suggested) were merged. The tasks were checked again on 2026-11-15 against that state with the same walk-through: where a task says "Will it hold?", read "Strength analysis"; tasks changed by the re-check say so. Still as found after the merge (seen again): T-395, T-396, T-397, T-399, T-400, T-404, T-407 (13 × "The CAD model" in `rules.yaml`), T-413, T-456. Not run again after the merge: the tool runs behind T-401, T-405, T-406, T-410, T-436, T-442 and the numbers of groups F, G and L (engine code; the merged work was in `web/`).

**What already works, keep it:** boot 3–5 s and a sample loads in 2–3 s; no account, no upload; the working bar (what runs, time left, what it updates, Cancel); finding cards (plain title, one-line reason, fix chip, tap shows the place); the 3MF with settings on the part; the calm look (DESIGN.md).

**Hosting costs nothing for the simulations:** every calculation runs in the visitor's browser (Pyodide in a web worker). The host only serves static files; the Python runtime (~15 MB) and scipy (~10 MB) come from a public CDN once. T-448 writes this down for users.

**Groups.** Each task carries a group letter in brackets. Tasks of one group touch the same files and ideas, so one agent can take a whole group on one branch per task, one after another, without colliding with another group. Suggested order: **A** first (small fixes, each under an hour), then **B** and **C** (what the tool says must be right, and the first minute), then **F**, **G**, **H**. **D** is a rebuild of the navigation and needs the user's yes first (it reverses parts of T-375 and T-378). Inside a group the tasks are in build order.

| Group | Theme | Main files | Tasks |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | Quick fixes, each small and alone | `web/index.html`, `web/app.js`, `web/style.css` | T-395 – T-404 |
| B | Advice that must be right: orientation, finding text, organic shapes | `core/fdmcore/orientation.py`, `api.py`, `checks.py`, `data/rules.yaml` | T-405 – T-410 |
| C | The first minute: start, Simple flow, result screen | `web/index.html`, `web/app.js`, `web/steps.js` | T-411 – T-416, T-465 |
| D | Navigation rebuild: two modes, a short spine and a toolbox, smaller code files | `web/steps.js`, `web/app.js`, `web/index.html` | T-417 – T-420 |
| E | The 3D view shows the problem | `web/viewer.js`, `web/app.js` | T-421 – T-423 |
| F | Slicer settings: made for this part, shown as a difference, handed to the slicer | `core/fdmcore/recommend.py`, `api.py`, `web/app.js` | T-424 – T-431 |
| G | Design changes: tied to a problem, checked after applying, advice with numbers | `core/fdmcore/features.py`, `additions.py`, `web/app.js`, `web/geometry.js` | T-432 – T-441 |
| H | Strength results: honest and proven | `web/app.js`, `web/viewer.js`, `core/fdmcore/strength.py`, `api.py` | T-442 – T-445 |
| I | From the printed part back into the tool | `web/index.html`, `web/app.js`, `core/fdmcore/api.py` | T-446 – T-447 |
| J | Reach: publish, install, printers, slicers, languages, projects | README, `web/`, `core/fdmcore/data/` | T-448 – T-455 |
| K | Phone layout | `web/style.css`, `web/index.html` | T-456 – T-457 |
| L | Big and broken parts: speed, memory, start-up | `core/fdmcore/`, `web/worker.js`, `web/app.js` | T-458 – T-461 |
| M | Tests that would have caught this | `core/tests/`, `tools/` | T-462 – T-464, T-466 |

**Decisions (2026-11-15):** no phone app, the phone only reads reports: decided (T-457, done). Move away what the slicer does better: decided, yes to the proposed list (T-431, now a build task). Two modes instead of four levels (T-417) and the bigger 3D view on part steps (T-465): put off until relevant; group D waits.

### F. Slicer settings: made for this part

- [x] **T-424 P1** [F] Walls, infill and top layers from the part's shape, not only from the goal. · files: `core/fdmcore/recommend.py` (`goal_plan`, `recommend_settings`), `core/fdmcore/checks.py` (wall thickness stats), `core/fdmcore/data/thresholds.yaml`, `core/tests/` · owner: Codex GPT-6.1 Sol, reviewed by Claude (`task/T-424-shape-settings`); shares `checks.py` / `thresholds.yaml` with T-408 (separate blocks) · outcome: Shells follow measured walls and interior: walls sized to fill thin parts ('infill hardly used here'), an extra loop for mostly-wall parts, +5/+10 % infill for 30/100 cm³ interiors, one more top layer over <=10 % infill, Arachne from measured in-between walls, each with a reason naming the part. Not met on purpose: 3 different (walls, infill, top) triples across the 20 parts, not 6; the extra variety only came from lowering infill on small hooks and brackets, which the review removed. Engine 411 passed, browser smoke 88 passed / 0 failed.
  - Seen: across 21 different parts (PLA, P1S, Balanced): layer height 0.2 on all 21, infill 15 % gyroid on all 21, top / bottom 4 / 3 on all 21, elephant foot 0.15 on all 21, walls 3 on 19 and 4 on 2. Only on/off extras (supports, brim, ironing, bridge speed, hole compensation) react to the part. The core values come from the table `GOAL_PLANS`.
  - Do: measure from the slices: the typical and thinnest wall thickness, the share of the volume that is thick (would be infill), the top area. Then: (1) a part whose walls are ≤ 2 × walls × line width is solid anyway: say so, set walls so they fill it exactly, and call infill "hardly used here". (2) A thick block: fewer walls don't help; infill carries: pick density from the goal and the block's size. (3) Low infill (≤ 10 %) → one more top layer so the top closes. (4) Thin walls that are not a whole number of lines → Arachne (already there) and the reason. Keep the goal as the starting point.
  - Done when: the 21 parts give at least 6 different (walls, infill, top) triples; each change carries a reason (T-426); tests pin three typical parts (thin shell, thick block, mixed).

  - Why: the tool edits a triangle mesh by cutting and adding shapes built from flat slices. That is reliable on flat-sided features and fragile on curved or detailed ones, and it never gives a real CAD feature: the user redoes it in CAD anyway. What a designer needs is the number and the place.
  - Do: every design fix exists as advice: a label on the part and a list line, e.g. "Make this hole Ø3.4 mm (now Ø3.0)", "Make this wall 1.35 mm = 3 lines (now 1.0)", "Open this gap to 0.3 mm (now 0.15)", "Round this inside corner with r 2 mm". "Copy the list" for the CAD session, and it goes into the report. Auto-apply stays for the short proven list (teardrop, corner rounding, ledge fill, sacrificial layer, thicker thin wall, sized hole / post / slot), each guarded by T-434.
  - Done when: every finding with a design fix shows a number and a place, whether or not it can be applied.
  - Done 2026-11-20 (Gemini 4 Neon, reviewed by Claude): "Numbers for CAD" list in Design changes, on the part (number labels, click to show where), in reports and copyable: every design fix with its exact number, from the edit itself or, with no edit, only from the finding's metrics, the profile and thresholds.yaml (no number, no line). Identical lines are grouped ("4 bed corners: round with r 3.0 mm"); holes with a screw suggestion get no separate hole size (no Ø3.0 vs Ø2.5 clash on `enclosure`). Advice on 19 of 25 sample and corpus parts.

### H. Strength results: honest and proven

- [x] **T-442 P1** [H] Bands instead of "×17.7". · files: `web/app.js` (`renderStrength`, `compute`, `renderVersions`), `core/fdmcore/api.py` (`strength` verdict text) · owner: Codex GPT-6.1 Terra, reviewed by Claude (`task/T-442-strength-bands`)
  - Why: the check uses about 14,000 cubes of 1 mm and material values not yet checked against prints. One decimal promises a precision it doesn't have.

  - Done when: no headline shows a decimal safety factor above ×10.
