Written into PRODUCT.md ("Operating Context"): on a phone the site has to do two things well, read a shared report and the failed-print path (T-447); the rest only has to work. T-456 is sized to that.
  - Why: prints started from a phone are one-tap, pre-sliced models (e.g. Bambu Handy, about 1.6 M installs; no public number for the share of prints) and need no check. A part that needs more than a default profile is at a desk, where the CAD or slicer file is. Simulations on a phone would be slow (not measured).
  - Proposal: no native app. The phone layout must do two things well: read a shared report, and later the failed-print path at the printer (T-447). Editing loads, fits and design changes on a phone is not a goal.
  - Done when: written into PRODUCT.md ("Operating Context").

  - Seen in code: the ~35 engine files are fetched one after another, each with `?v=<time>`, so nothing is ever cached by the browser, on every start.
  - Do: fetch in parallel (`Promise.all`), and use a content hash per file in the manifest instead of the time, so unchanged files come from the cache; or ship the engine as one zip that Pyodide unpacks. Measure the start on a slow connection (browser dev tools "Slow 4G") before and after.

The hashes are written only by the Pages build (`make_manifest.py --hashes`); the committed manifest stays a file list, so engine edits need no re-run and a local server always serves fresh code (fetched in parallel, not cached). Headless Edge via `http.server` on Slow 4G (400 kbit/s, 400 ms latency via CDP on page + workers): first visit 49.60 s → 34.58 s (engine fetch 31.69 s → 16.09 s); second visit 39.59 s → 7.41 s with 0 engine files fetched from the network (was 36); unthrottled second visit 5.16 s → 5.20 s with 0 engine files fetched.

## Thermal optimizer (2026-11-18, tag [TO])

Start these only after the open tasks above are done. Background: [research/thermal_optimizer_plan.md](research/thermal_optimizer_plan.md)
(the original plan). The review on 2026-11-18 changed that plan: no server, no accounts and no uploads. The engine is a normal `fdmcore` module:
browser (Pyodide: numpy, scipy, shapely, pyyaml only, so no Numba, lib3mf, libbgcode or pyGCodeDecode) plus local mode / MCP.
**Rule for every [TO] task:** don't drop a feature, metric or quality aspect because it's slow or its constants are unknown. Use an approximation
from published sources, show how sure we are, and tune it later. Handle speed with tiers (quick / fine) and finer detail where it matters.
Order: T-470 → T-477. T-477 (real prints) decides whether to go on to T-480 and later tasks. serves: 2, 3, 4.

- [x] **T-470 P2** [TO] Thermal constants from published sources. · files: `core/fdmcore/data/thermal.yaml` (new), `research/thermal_constants.md` (new) · owner: —
  - Do: for PLA, PETG, ABS/ASA, TPU, PA and PC, collect: density, heat capacity c_p(T) with a latent-heat peak for semi-crystalline plastics (PA, PP, PEEK; PETG is amorphous), thermal conductivity k, emissivity, Tg, the temperature where a printed layer stops sagging, and parameters for welding layers together (reptation / WLF, e.g. Seppala et al. 2017 *Soft Matter*, Coogan & Kazmer 2017 *Rapid Prototyping J.*; check both before citing). Per printer type: heat transfer from the fan vs fan % (with spin-up delay), contact resistance between beads and to the bed, nozzle and heater-block reheating, air temperature for open / passive / heated enclosures, melt temperature drop at high flow. Gemini can do the bulk reading.
  - Every value records its source, a confidence level (`published` / `estimated` / `tuned`) and a range.
  - Done when: every constant T-473 and T-474 need has a value, a source and a confidence level.
  - Outcome: `core/fdmcore/data/thermal.yaml` (7 materials, process constants) and research/thermal_constants.md. Gemini drafted; every DOI checked with Crossref and the open papers read: 5 of Gemini's 11 DOIs were fake, those values are `estimated` now. Published: ABS WLF (Seppala 2017: 4.65 / 200.9 at 230 C), PLA c_p (Pyda 2004), Costa 2014 convection bracket and emissivity, density / Tg from datasheets. Estimated, to tune in T-477 / T-482: reptation times, WLF for all but ABS / ASA, fan h by fan %, contact conductances, melt drop by flow. Not yet loaded by any code (T-473).

    - speed (F) for each line, travel moves, retractions;
    - fan commands (`M106/M107 P*`, Klipper `SET_FAN_SPEED`);
    - pressure-advance changes (`M900`, `SET_PRESSURE_ADVANCE`);
    - object ID (`EXCLUDE_OBJECT` / `M486`) and tool changes;
    - firmware type (Marlin / Klipper / RRF / Bambu);
    - the slicer settings block: PrusaSlicer/Orca at the end, Bambu `CONFIG_BLOCK` at the top, `.gcode.3mf` `project_settings.config`. Check with fixture files from each slicer.
  - Slowdown causes: for each line printed slower than its feature's set speed, record why: `overhang`, `bridge`, `small_perimeter`, `volumetric_cap`, `layer_time` or `unknown`. Only `layer_time` slowdowns may be undone later.
  - Read big files (50–500 MB) in chunks without holding the whole text in memory. `spot_heat` and the existing detailed checks keep working.
  - Done when: fixtures from Orca, Bambu Studio and PrusaSlicer parse with correct speeds, fans and slowdown causes; a 200 MB file stays under a set memory limit.
  - Outcome: `Toolpath` now also carries:

    - firmware and the slicer's settings (from the G-code block, or the `.gcode.3mf` project_settings.config).

- [x] **T-472 P2** [TO] Print timing: when each line is printed and where the nozzle is. · files: `core/fdmcore/kinematics.py` (new) · owner: Claude (task/T-472-print-timing)
  - Do: a numpy model of speeding up and slowing down (trapezoid), with look-ahead. It covers Marlin jerk / junction deviation and Klipper `square_corner_velocity` / `minimum_cruise_ratio`; Bambu is approximated and checked against the slicer's own estimate. Output: start and end time of each line and the nozzle position over time, which T-473 needs for the fan and nozzle heat.
  - Done when: total time is within ~5–10 % of the slicer's estimate on the fixtures, and within a few % against real print times once T-477 has them.
  - Outcome: `kinematics.timing(tp)` plans every move like the firmware: trapezoid speed profiles, look-ahead as running minimums (no Python loop), corners by Marlin jerk / junction deviation / Klipper square_corner_velocity + minimum_cruise_ratio.
    The limits are the G-code's own commands (M204/M205/M201/M203/M566, SET_VELOCITY_LIMIT; now recorded by `gcode.parse`), else the machine settings. Retractions and G4 pauses stop the nozzle and take their time. `nozzle_at(tp, tm, times)` gives the nozzle position.
    Against the slicers' own estimates on the T-471 fixtures: OrcaSlicer −6 % (201 vs 214 s), PrusaSlicer −2 %, Bambu Studio +1 %. Feed rates alone are 20–30 % too fast.
    It plans in pieces of 200 k moves, cut where the nozzle stops: 600 k lines take 0.3 s natively and in Pyodide, peak 89 MB. Engine tests 665 passed (no web/api change). Real print times: T-477.
- [x] **T-473 P2** [TO] Heat model (tier A), in the browser and locally. · files: `core/fdmcore/thermal.py` (new), `web/worker.js` (`NEEDS_SCIPY`), `tools/make_manifest.py` · owner: Claude (task/T-473-heat-model)
  - Do (numpy + `scipy.sparse`):
    - **Grid:** draw each layer's lines onto a 0.5–1 mm grid; islands = connected areas; columns = islands linked from layer to layer.
    - **Heat network:** one node per 2–4 mm cell. Plastic is added at melt temperature (minus the drop at high flow), at the time and place it is printed. Heat flows sideways, down (with contact resistance) and into the bed. The model includes:
      - fan cooling that depends on fan %, its spin-up delay and the nozzle's distance;
      - reheating from the nozzle;
      - radiation;
      - air temperature by enclosure and height;
      - c_p(T) and latent heat.
    - **Joint between layers:** a small model of the joint gives its temperature over time T_int(t) for each new node.
    - **Solver:** implicit and event-driven. The top 20–30 layers are kept in full detail; older layers are merged.
  - Two tiers, both with every part of the model: **quick** (browser, aim ~60 s for a typical small part, with progress) and **fine** (smaller cells, more layers at full detail, used locally or as a longer browser run). Big parts: fine detail only around hot spots and important regions (T-475).
  - Check with `node tools/pyodide_check.mjs`; run `make_manifest.py`.
  - Done when: it matches or beats `gcode.spot_heat` and `heat.py` on the test shapes; quick tier within its time budget; T-477 measurements fall inside an agreed error band.
  - Outcome: `thermal.simulate(tp, timing, profile, tier)`, behind `api.heat_model` / MCP `heat_model` (scipy in the worker). Each layer's plastic is drawn into 2 mm (quick) or 1 mm (fine) cells:
    - each cell is born at nozzle temperature minus the flow drop, at its real print time (T-472);
    - heat flows sideways (and across 45° infill corners), down through contact resistance, and into the bed;
    - losses: still air plus the part fan (%, spin-up, distance below the nozzle), radiation, air warmer near the bed (new `air_bed_layer_mm` in thermal.yaml, estimated), nozzle reheating;
    - c_p(T) and latent heat; slivers merge into their fullest neighbour.
    Implicit solver with warm-started conjugate gradients. Only the top 25 / 60 layers stay in detail; older ones merge into one lump per column.
    Per node: temperature and seconds until covered, joint T_int at 0.5–64 s, seconds above Tg, islands and columns.
    Checks:
    - matches the exact cooling of a plate within 3 %;
    - on the fixture, the pin is covered at ~150 °C vs ~80 °C for the block, ranked the same as `spot_heat`;
    - quick and fine tiers agree on cover temperature within 10 %.
    Timing: a 20-min print takes 13 s in Pyodide and 6 s natively; a 73-min print 30 s natively.
    Engine tests 674 passed, full browser test 0 failures, pyodide_check ok. Not done yet: the error band against real prints (T-477).
- [x] **T-474 P2** [TO] Temperature window and goals. · files: `core/fdmcore/thermal.py`, `core/fdmcore/data/rules.yaml`, `core/fdmcore/api.py` · owner: Claude (task/T-474-temperature-window)
  - Do: each node gets a window with two limits:
    - **Too hot (shape):** temperature of the layer below when the next bead lands ≤ the sag limit, stricter on overhangs and bridges.
    - **Too cold (bonding):** seconds the joint stays above Tg ≥ a minimum; add the welding integral with WLF / reptation values from T-470.
  - Goals: **Fastest / Balanced / Strongest**, which move the window. Design-intent tags (cosmetic / load-bearing / dimension-critical) adjust it per region.
  - New findings ("spot stays too hot", "spot is overcooled, weak bonding", "slowdown that isn't needed") need `rules.yaml` entries, each with a slicer or process fix, in plain words.
  - Done when: the thin pin is flagged too hot, a thick load-bearing wall under 100 % fan is flagged too cold, and a block passes.
  - Outcome: `thermal.window(heat, profile, intents)` gives every spot two limits:
    - **too hot:** temperature when covered above the layer heat check's limit (softening + hot margin), or above the material's `no_sag_c` on bridges and overhangs;
    - **too cold:** joint healing below a minimum. Healing is a welding integral: the WLF-shifted reptation time over the joint's temperature (Wool's ¼ power), including the moment the bead lands (contact at the mean temperature for ~0.1 s, which the cells can't resolve).
    Each column is judged in 5-layer bands by the median. A slowdown is needless where a band is too cold and the slicer slowed it for layer cooling.
    Profile goals (speed / material / balanced / strength) and design intents (cosmetic / dimension_critical / load_bearing; strength loads count as load-bearing) move the limits; values are in `thermal.yaml` `window`.
    The healing scale rests on reptation times known only to ~10×, so the limits are anchored on the fixtures: Ø3 mm pin too hot; Orca and Bambu default-cooling blocks pass; a load-bearing 6 mm wall at 100 % fan too cold (same wall at 30 % passes).
    New rules `heat_too_hot`, `heat_weak_bond`, `heat_needless_slowdown`, each with slicer fixes. `api.heat_model` / MCP `heat_model` return the findings and a per-spot window code.
    Engine tests 682 passed, full browser test 0 failures. T-477 / T-482 tune the limits against real prints.
- [x] **T-475 P2** [TO] Strength guides the priorities. · files: `core/fdmcore/thermal.py`, `core/fdmcore/strength.py` (`failure_indices`), `core/fdmcore/api.py` · owner: Claude (task/T-475-strength-priorities)
  - Do: a weight for each node from three inputs:
    - the strength check's "Layer" index (tension across layers, shear along them) for the user's loads;
    - design-intent tags;
    - when no loads are entered, guessed importance: thin tall features and pins, sharp inside corners, roots of ribs and pegs, screw and insert holes, outer walls over infill. Mark it "guessed" and link to "Add loads".
  - The weight makes the window stricter where a spot matters, and it sets where the fine detail goes. Weights never go below a minimum, so every spot keeps the "doesn't sag" limit; only the bonding goal is relaxed on unimportant spots.
  - Done when: on a bracket with a load, the root gets the highest weight; with no loads, the fallback picks the pins and roots on the T-389 test part.
  - Outcome: new `importance.py`: a weight from 0.2 to 1 per spot.
    - **With loads:** `strength.layer_index` (the "Layer" index of `failure_indices`, highest over the load cases) × the profile's required safety, so a spot right at that safety weighs 1. Cubes right at loads and supports are capped at the highest value elsewhere.
    - **Without loads,** guessed from the shape and marked `guessed` ("Add the loads for a real answer"): roots of upright pins, pegs and ribs 1.0 (thin sections that end in the air and stand on wider plastic), the features themselves 0.9, sharp inside corners and screw / insert holes 0.7, outer walls 0.5, infill inside 0.2.
    - **Intents:** spots marked load-bearing weigh 1.
    - The weight moves only the bonding goal of `thermal.window` (the goal's own `min_bond` at 0.5, ×0.5 at 0.2, +0.35 at 1, the same as marking load-bearing); the sag limit stays for every spot. Values in `thermal.yaml` `importance` (estimated; T-477 tunes them).
    - `api.heat_model` returns `importance`, `importance_reason` and `guessed` per spot. A heat cell takes its most important plastic: the middle of a bent peg carries nothing, its sides the most. A weak bond is a warning only where the loads or a load-bearing mark say so; a guessed spot gets a hint with the reason.
    - New `api.importance` / MCP `spot_importance`: per-cube weights, reasons and the important `areas` in plain words.
    - Checks: on the L-bracket pushed at the top of its upright, the upright's root weighs most (0.92); on the feature test part with no loads, the roots of the Ø3 pin and the thin slot walls come first. A sideways hole's side walls aren't taken for ribs.

- [x] **T-480 P2** [TO] The optimizer. · files: `core/fdmcore/thermal_opt.py` (new) · owner: Claude (task/T-480-thermal-optimizer) · user (2026-11-21): build it on the estimated limits now, check against real prints later (T-477 is no longer a gate)
  - Do:
    - **Sliding window:** look ahead 5–10 layers, fix the current layer, move on.
    - **What it costs:** time + Σ weight × how far each spot is outside its window + penalties for speed jumps on outer walls, speeds in vibration (VFA) bands, and many fan changes.
    - **Fixes, cheapest first:** turn the fan up ahead of time; slow other islands; slow the hot island (only when nozzle reheating isn't the main heat source); a cooling park move or tower. When a spot is too cold: lower the fan or skip an unneeded slowdown.
    - **Never touched:** overhang, bridge, small-perimeter and unknown slowdowns; layers 0–1; travel; wipe and ironing; prime/purge towers.
    - **Fans:** ignore fan changes shorter than ~2× the spin-up time; respect kick-start; don't turn the fan off mid-layer unless the slicer did.
    - **Big parts:** prioritise by weight when not everything can be fixed. Total speed-up is capped (e.g. 30 %, Expert can raise it).
  - Done when: on the T-477 parts it is faster than Orca's cooling slowdown at equal or better window and strength results (simulated, then confirmed with a print).
  - Outcome: new `thermal_opt.py`, `api.optimize_cooling` / MCP `optimize_cooling`. Built on the estimated limits (user, 2026-11-21); T-477 / T-482 check them later.
    - **How:** the heat model takes tens of seconds per run, so the plan is chosen with a cooling law fitted per spot to two runs: as sliced, and with the part fan far from the slicer's. It is anchored on the real run, and bonds move between the two runs.
    - **Per layer, from the bottom, cheapest first** (thermal.yaml `optimizer`):
      - a fan value, starting 3 spin-ups early and holding at least 3 layers;
      - slower lines (×0.85 … 0.4);
      - undoing the slicer's layer-time slowdown (×1.5 … up to the set speed; total speed-up ≤ 30 %);
      - a cooling park move (3–24 s beside the part, before the covering layer: slowing a tiny pin further mostly keeps the hot nozzle over it).
      The cost is time + importance weight × how far each column's median is outside its window. Neighbouring layers' speeds ramp (≤ ×1.25 apart).
    - **Checked with the heat model and corrected once:** still too hot → the next stronger fix; made too cold → one step back (whichever weighs more).
    - **Never touched:** overhang / bridge / small-perimeter / flow-cap / unknown slowdowns, layers 0–1, non-part lines, ironing and wipes; nothing faster than its set speed.
    - **Two plans, each simulated:** `gcode` (fan, speeds, parks per layer, for T-481) and `slicer` (slower heights + a cooling tower sized like the longest park, as `export(cooling=…)` takes it, T-479). `summary` in plain words, including what can't be fixed ("a feature that thin can't do both").
    - **Checks:**
      - load-bearing 6 mm wall slowed 200 → 20 mm/s: 32 % faster (cap), median bond 0.45 → 0.49, nothing too hot;
      - Orca fixture pin: 40 spots too hot → 0 (3 s parks, +22 % time), nothing too cold;
      - Bambu fixture, guessed weights (pin weighs ~1): 40 → 30 too hot, 0 too cold. The rest can't be fixed without weak bonds.
      Pyodide: 21 s for a 7.5 min print.
    - **Not done:** slowing other islands before the hot one (all adjustable lines of a layer slow together), vibration (VFA) bands, kick-start. The real-print confirmation is T-477.
- [x] **T-481 P2** [TO] Option B (opt-in): change the user's G-code directly. · files: `core/fdmcore/gcode_rewrite.py` (new), `core/fdmcore/api.py`, `web/app.js` · owner: Claude (task/T-481-gcode-rewrite)
  - Do: the user picks "Optimize my G-code directly", loads their file, and downloads the result; the original always stays available.
    - Change only speed (F) and add fan commands. Never change positions, extrusion (E) or temperatures. Split a line where the speed must change partway.
    - Re-emit adaptive pressure advance for the new speed (or keep changes within ±X % if there's no PA table). Recompute `M73`. Use each firmware's fan commands (Marlin / Klipper / Bambu). The header names the changes and the time before → after.
    - **Check before download:** same E per layer, same paths, same bounding box, speeds within machine limits, flow under the cap, no outer walls in vibration bands, and a re-simulation no worse anywhere. If a check fails, don't offer the file and say why.
    - Plain `.gcode` first; `.gcode.3mf` repack (plate gcode, md5, `slice_info.config`) once tested on a real printer.
  - Done when: an optimized pin-pair G-code passes all checks and prints; a broken rewrite is refused.
  - Outcome: new `gcode_rewrite.py`, `api.optimize_gcode(up, data, …)` and MCP `optimize_gcode_file`.
    - **What changes:** only feed rates (the modal F is put back for the lines after a changed one), the part fan (`M106 S…`, `M106 P1 S…` on Bambu), and cooling park moves (retract, travel beside the part, `G4`, back, unretract; relative or absolute E). Positions, E and temperatures stay. The plan changes whole layers, so no line needs splitting.
    - **Around it:** `M73` progress is recomputed (the start G-code is left alone, minutes-left scaled from the slicer's own). A header goes after the slicer's header block. The parser now records each line's source text line (`Toolpath.src`).
    - **Pressure advance:** with adaptive pressure advance (no table to redo it), speed changes stay within ±25 %.
    - **Check before handing it out** (`gcode_rewrite.check`): read back; same paths, plastic per layer and bounding box; speeds within the machine's X/Y limits and the filament's flow cap; then a heat simulation of the new file, where no spot may end up outside its window that wasn't before. Otherwise `ok: false` with the reasons, and no file.
    - The flow cap is also enforced in the optimizer now (`gcode.flow_cap`): undoing a slowdown never needs more plastic per second than the filament melts. The first try failed the check because of this.
    - **Results:** OrcaSlicer fixture: pin 40 spots too hot → 0, 4.7 → 5.7 min, 1,107 lines' speed, 19 parks, all checks pass. A tampered E, a moved path or a speed above the limit is refused. Pyodide: 16 s.
    - **Not done:** vibration-band check (no VFA data yet); `.gcode.3mf` repack (waits for a real-printer test); printing the result (T-477). The "Optimize my G-code directly" button belongs to the new UI (moved to T-478).

The smooth blob then got 30 ledge fills, which on a sculpt would reshape it (and together pinch the mesh, T-490): ledge fills now follow the CAD-checks switch (`api.CAD_ONLY_EDITS`), and the app re-suggests design changes when the switch is flipped. New test `test_organic_part_gets_no_ledge_fills_until_cad_checks_are_on`.

## UI revision (2026-11-21, tag [UI])

The owner rejected the current UI (feedback round 5). This section rebuilds the app as **one guided desktop flow**:
Start → Part → Setup → Environment → Holds → Loads → Load details → Analyse → Options → Print simulation → Problems & fixes → Done.
**Read first, every time:** [research/redesign-2026-11/PLAN-COMBINED.md](research/redesign-2026-11/PLAN-COMBINED.md) (the plan;
its **section 16 = the owner's decisions, which override everything else**) and [feedback.md](research/redesign-2026-11/feedback.md)
(the owner's words, verbatim). Each task below names the plan sections (§) it implements; the task line is a summary, the plan is the spec.
The three source plans (`plan-claude.md`, `plan-gpt.md`, `plan-gemini.md`) are background only.

Rules for every [UI] task:
- **Don't take inspiration from the current UI** (markup, CSS, wording). Build new in `web/ui/`, `web/steps/`, `web/viewport/`,
  `web/state.js`, `web/flow.js`; the old `app.js`/`steps.js` are replaced at cut-over (T-580), not refactored.
- Desktop only (≥ 1280 × 720), no `max-width` media queries. Header always in grid row 1. No level switch, no Expert page.
- Short text: labels ≤ 4 words, one hint line ≤ 80 chars, details behind "More"; all text via `t()` keys (AGENTS.md).
- Spacing only from the token scale (4/8/12/16/24/32/48); the lint test from T-510 must pass.
- Engine code stays Pyodide-safe (numpy, scipy, shapely, pyyaml); new API methods get their MCP tool in the same PR.
- Order: T-500 first; Phase 1 (style mockups) and Phase 2 (engine) in parallel; T-530 and later only after the owner approved T-512.
  First goal: one complete working path (sample → … → Export) before the extras.
- The thermal tasks T-472…T-484 continue unchanged and plug into Simulation (T-560) and Done (T-562, T-571).
- Superseded by this section (don't start them; close them when the matching [UI] task lands): "D. Navigation rebuild",
  "K. Phone layout", the level/step items under "Levels and steps", and the strength-form UI items under "Strength analysis".

### Phase 0: triage on today's UI

The old levels stay reachable only through `?level=` / `fdm-test-level` for the old browser test until T-580. Also fixed a race in `renderCopies` when a new part loads mid-plate. Full e2e green.

  - Do: apply intents on the original STEP with exact tools (cylinder/prism/box booleans first), `BRepCheck_Analyzer` after each op; an edit that fails falls back to its triangle pieces with a plain note; tessellate the result (with face ids) for display and the engine's re-check; write STEP. Free shapes; one worker.
  - Done when: the fixture's hole resize, teardrop, slab, void and rib come back as exact faces (face count stays near the original), a forced failure falls back, and the browser test downloads the edited STEP.
  - Outcome: `web/brep.js` `applyEdits` (used by `stepwrite-worker.js`, no new worker).
    - **Order:** inside-corner rounds and chamfers first, on the edge the two named B-rep faces share (T-527); then every addition, then every cut.
    - **Exact tools:** a prism from the flat outline, a cylinder ring, `BRepFilletAPI` fillets and chamfers. `BRepCheck_Analyzer` runs after each edit.
    - **Fallback:** an edit that fails, or has no intent, falls back to its sewn triangle pieces (the old path). One that fails that way too is left out. Each edit gets a note (exact / mesh / skipped, and why), and the old app's STEP download says which were faceted or left out.
    - **Output:** the worker now also returns the result tessellated with its face ids (`tessellate`), for showing it and checking it again. Hooking that into the design-change flow belongs to the new app's Problems step (T-561).
    - `piece_ops` now reach the worker too: grip fins used to lose them.
  - Measured on the L-bracket (10 faces), `tools/check_brep_edits.mjs` via `test_brep_edits.py`, exact vs forced mesh:
    - hole Ø6 → Ø6.9: 10 faces vs 265;
    - inside-corner round: 11 (a real fillet) vs 16;
    - teardrop 15, slab 16, void 17, rib 13 (the same faces as the mesh path: those shapes are flat anyway);
    - each 33–153 ms in Node; the same plastic either way;
    - a round too big to make falls back with "OpenCascade could not make the round";
    - all together: one valid solid, 45 faces, written and read back alike.
  - The old app's STEP download (and its browser round-trip check) went with T-580; the new app doesn't download a STEP with design changes yet, so `stepWithEdits`'s notes (`stepfile.*` messages) wait for that button. The Node checks above cover the worker's code.

  - Do: `fillet` / `chamfer` intents on the edge between two faces (T-527); radius from the engine's stress-corner edit; fallback to today's faceted wedge on failure.

  - Outcome: the corner edits' intents (T-528) are made by T-529's `applyEdits` with `BRepFilletAPI_MakeFillet` / `MakeChamfer`, on the edge the two named faces share. Mixed corners get the rounds first, then the chamfers. `brep.surfaces` reports each face's surface kind (and a cylinder's radius) for the checks.
    - `test_exact_fillets.py` (Node, via `check_brep_edits.mjs`):
      - the L-bracket's stress corner becomes one new cylindrical face with exactly the engine's radius;
      - printed upside down, the corner would hang over, so it becomes a 45° chamfer: one new flat face;

- [x] **T-535 P3** [UI] STEP corpus and robustness report for B-rep edits. · plan: §16.4; research/cad_engine.md ("Risks") · files: `core/tests/corpus_step/` (new), a report under `research/` · owner: Claude (`task/T-535-step-corpus`)
  - Do: 8–12 real-world STEP parts (open licences, sources noted); run every intent kind on each; record success rate, time and memory in a browser.

Hole resizes, slabs, voids, chamfers 100 % exact, teardrops 92 % (3 faceted: outline not flat); rounds 54 % exact, the rest left out and named in the status. All 15 parts write one valid solid with every edit together, 0.1–7.4 s, 100–811 MB. Verdict: every kind stays on.
    - Fixed on the way: a faceted fallback of dozens of pieces took a minute and 1.8 GB on a small knob and came out wrong. Now it is capped at 8 pieces and its volume change has to go the right way.
    - Rounds too wide for the faces beside them are retried at ½ and ¼ size (the note says so; message `stepfile.smaller` for when the new app downloads STEP files). Chamfers after rounds follow the edges the round trimmed, and one CAD edge gets one kind (the engine had asked for a round and a chamfer on the same edge).
    - Validity is checked before meshing, because BRepCheck trips over the triangles it adds. The final face merge is kept only if it stays valid. Left: weak-spot corners with many mixed edges on real parts still fail about half the time.

### Simulation accuracy (2026-11-23, from the owner's review of T-390 and the heat model)

Gaps found while reviewing T-390 / T-484 and the heat model. Real-print checks stay in T-477 / T-443; these make the
models better before that. Papers the owner supplied (PDFs, not in the repo): Laumann 2023 (PLA bed peel), Spoerk 2018
(bed temperature and adhesion), Costa 2014 (heat transfer conditions in FFF), Zabihollah 2024 (PLA notch factors).
Not reachable: Pollák & Goryl 2025.

- [x] **T-590 P1** Read the supplied papers and put their numbers into the research notes. · files: `research/bed_adhesion.md`, `research/fillet_stress_factors.md`, `research/thermal_constants.md` · owner: GPT-6.1 Terra, reviewed by Claude (`task/T-590-paper-numbers`)
  - Do: Laumann + Spoerk: grip per plastic, bed surface and bed temperature (and print speed); Costa: contact conductance, convection with and without fan; Zabihollah: measured notch factors vs fillet radius. Say what each number would replace in the code.
  - Outcome: Spoerk confirms the PLA shear anchors (glass 0.73 → 6.51, PI 0.51 → 3.22 N/mm at 60 → 70 °C; ABS keeps rising to 120 °C, PI above glass) → T-591 makes it a curve. Laumann's PLA 90° peel peaks are 3–9 N/mm of width, 10–100× the peel work T-390 derives from shear: the absolute grip scale is unproven, so calibrating from the warping bars (T-592) matters most. Costa only ran assumed cases (contact 0 / 100 / 10⁶, air 5 / 60 / 150 W/m²K): no measured replacement for the heat constants (T-596 stays an estimate; a fan source note was corrected). Zabihollah: filleted PLA coupons failed at about half the plain strength (apparent factor 1.85–2.22, no scatter given), against almost no loss in Seifollahi 2026: the fillet advice now says the benefit varies instead of "helps a little"; still no numeric factor.
- [x] **T-591 P2** Bed grip from the new data, smooth with bed temperature. · files: `core/fdmcore/warp.py`, `core/fdmcore/data/thresholds.yaml` · owner: GPT-6.2 Sol, reviewed by Claude (`task/T-591-grip-curve`)
  - Do: replace T-390 estimates where T-590 found measurements; grip as a smooth curve around each plastic's softening point instead of the 60 / 70 °C step, so "raise the bed 5 °C" can be judged.
  - Outcome: PLA on glass and PI follows measured shear points with labelled extrapolation. Other combinations vary with the plastic's softening temperature and keep their editable grip at the preset bed temperature. The adhesion result includes the bed setpoint. The 40 mm ABS bar holds and the 90 mm bar peels in native Python and Pyodide.
- [x] **T-592 P1** Calibrate bed grip from the warping test bars. · files: `core/fdmcore/warp.py`, `core/fdmcore/api.py`, `web/steps/settings.js` · owner: GPT-6.2 Sol, reviewed by Claude (`task/T-592-grip-calibration`)
  - Do: the user's "longest bar that stayed flat" (warping calibration print) → solve backwards for the grip on their plastic, plate and bed temperature; stored per material, replaces the estimate.
  - The warping test saves grip by material and enclosure for the recorded plate and bed temperature. Simulation uses a matching fit and keeps its estimate for one-sided or incompatible results. Open-printer ABS on textured PEI at 100 °C fits at 0.035 N/mm when 40 mm holds and 80 mm lifts. The current peel model cannot fit the 80 mm held and 120 mm lifted result: its release rate barely changes with bar length (the T-390 saturation), so longer bars need T-593 before they calibrate. At another bed temperature the fit scales with T-591's curve (the calibration sets the level, the curve the change).
- [x] **T-593 P2** Glue-layer peel model at the bed (cohesive zone). · files: `core/fdmcore/warp.py`, `core/fdmcore/fea.py` · owner: GPT-6.2 Sol, reviewed by Claude (`task/T-593-cohesive-peel`)
  - Do: the first layer held by springs that weaken and break as they stretch, so a lift grows by itself; must tell the short and long reference bars apart without T-390's curl gate (then drop the gate). Only in the detailed warping run if it is slow.
  - Outcome: First-layer springs weaken and break under shrinkage. The 40 mm ABS bar holds and the 90 mm bar starts to lift. The default-grid calibration reproduces 80 mm held and 120 mm lifted in Python and Pyodide.
- [x] **T-594 P2** Check the peel while printing, not only at the end. · files: `core/fdmcore/warp.py`, `core/fdmcore/api.py` (adhesion finding), `web/steps/simulate.js` (onset note) · owner: GPT-6.2 Sol, reviewed by Claude (`task/T-594-peel-while-printing`)
  - Do: run the peel check at each warp step (corners usually lift in the first layers, bed hot, part still soft); report the height where it starts.
- [x] **T-595 P2** Heated chamber: the real set point and its warm-up. · files: `core/fdmcore/thermal.py`, `core/fdmcore/data/thermal.yaml`, `core/fdmcore/warp.py` · owner: GPT-6.2 Sol, reviewed by Claude (`task/T-595-chamber-warmup`)
  - Do: read the chamber temperature from the G-code / printer profile instead of a fixed 60 °C; enclosures (passive and heated) warm up over the first ~30 min instead of being warm from layer 1.

- [x] **T-622 P2** Cooling plan: the rewritten G-code must match the plan. · files: `core/fdmcore/thermal_opt.py`, `core/fdmcore/gcode_rewrite.py` · owner: GPT-6.2 Sol (`task/T-595-chamber-warmup`)

### Phase 10: owner testing feedback (2026-11-24)

## Done

  - Outcome: `thermal.simulate(snapshots=True)` keeps every spot's temperature when each layer is done (uint8, at most 150 layers; about 90 KB on the test part), for the run as sliced and the G-code plan's run (`api._replay`). The heat panel opens on **Replay**: 1 · Watch it print (beads coloured set / good to build on / too soft, clock, layer, fan; as sliced or with the plan), 2 · What we found (the worst layers, each with Show), 3 · What we change (the plan in sentences, Replay with the plan). Logic in `heatlogic.js` (Node-tested), e2e checks the story, the jump and that the plan's replay differs.
- [x] Fixed a T-110 regression: grid-snapping layer outlines crashed Pyodide's GEOS on the L-bracket; now only invalid outlines are repaired (`tools/pyodide_check.mjs` added).
- [x] **T-110 P1** Fix GEOS topology exception in Pyodide on real models; dynamic goal presets (walls/infill/layer height); camera focus & 3D highlight on clicked findings; orientation box clearing; WIP yellow frames on unfinished features. · owner: Antigravity (branch `task/T-110-ux-fixes`)
- [x] Phase 0–2: engine, Pyodide app, orientation, pseudo-slicer checks, knowledge base, settings, 3MF/STL export, voxel FEA strength, strength-aware orientation.
- [x] Phase 3 (partial): hole detection, teardrop + corner chamfer edits with ghost preview.
- [x] Phase 4: MCP server, copy-report, Ask-AI panel (Anthropic + Gemini, own key).
- [x] STEP import with CAD faces; build-volume check / turn-to-fit.
- [x] Orientation fix for complex parts (strong penalty for no/tiny bed contact; hide bad options); typical-part regression tests.
- [x] Browser smoke test (`tools/e2e_smoke.py`) with optional Gemini screenshot review.
- [x] Impact-based findings (fails / shape / surface / weak / info): supports and overhangs no longer reported on their own; new checks: warping by material+enclosure, holes that close or print undersized, gaps that fuse, first-layer detail, rough fine detail, upright pins, necks, trapped support, bridge sag. Thresholds in `data/thresholds.yaml`.
- [x] Setup: multi-material option, goal (balanced/strength/speed/material), nozzle comparison; findings grouped by impact.
